# DESIGN §32 — 0.3.0

Part of [DESIGN.md](../DESIGN.md). The plugin system (插件).

## 32. The plugin system

Released 2026-10-14. The owner decided on 2026-10-12 that the plugin system is the 0.3.0 release. Its design study (the
maintainers' notes of 2026-10-12) proposed a phased plan; the owner adopted its recommendations with the decisions
below. This chapter records what each phase changes; the reference is [PLUGINS.md](../PLUGINS.md). §32.14 records
the fixes planned for 0.2.7, which the owner folded into 0.3.0 on 2026-10-14, and the passenger carriers (§32.14.4).

### 32.1 Decisions and phases

The owner's decisions (2026-10-12):

- **MVP:** cosmetic plugins (art) and data plugins, the host's switches in the room, a content hash that every client
  checks — and **no third-party code**. Code comes later in steps, each a decision of its own: a declarative DSL, then a
  sandbox, then trusted code with the explicit consent of the server owner and of every player.
- **Position:** the original game is always the default; plugins are marked **unofficial** everywhere; plugin matches
  stay out of the statistics overview by default — those with plugins that change gameplay: a match with only cosmetic
  plugins counts as the original game (the owner's decision of 2026-10-13, §32.3); bugs seen in a plugin room go to the
  plugin's author first; no plugin
  overrides arbitrary project files; the interface word is 「插件」 (never 「模组」, which names the operators' modules).
- Everything else as the study recommended: plugins are installed on the server only and chosen by the room's host
  (gameplay) or by a player for themself (cosmetics); `plugins/<id>/plugin.json`; `packs/` keeps working; one content
  set per room with a worker per modded set; per-plugin, per-room and concurrent-set limits; incompatible gameplay
  plugins refused by default; no official files distributed through plugins.

| Phase | Content | State |
|---|---|---|
| P0 | content identity and the plugin registry, no change for players | §32.2 |
| P1a | cosmetic plugins (room and personal) | §32.3 |
| P1b | data plugins: patches, compact records, kitRef / SkillSpec operators, modes, i18n | §32.5 |
| P1c | per-room content sets: the host's 插件 panel, a worker per set, the client loading and checking `/cs/<cs>/`, the unofficial marks | §32.6 |
| P2 | DSL-lite | §32.7 |
| P3 | one script API, trusted then sandboxed | §32.10 (SDK/native), §32.11 (sandbox/Worker/CSP) |
| P4 | interface plugins: a sandboxed frame, a read-only postMessage feed, personal switches | §32.8 |
| studio | the plugin studio and the authoring tools (part 1: cosmetic plugins, the error catalogue, schemas, hot reload) | §32.9 |

### 32.2 P0 — content identity and the registry

Seen: the design study's §6.1 and its "P0 基础" list. Nothing a player sees changes; the golden results are byte for
byte unchanged (no scenario moved).

**The content hash.** `cs` names the content a room plays (`shared/plugins/identity.js`):
`sha256(canonical({ v: 1, app, sim, data, plugins, settings }))`. `sim` hashes the sorted `[path, sha256]` list of
every served `/sim/**.js` (not `nodeData.js`), `/shared/**.js` and the `/data.js` stand-in; `data` maps every gameplay
`data/<name>.json` to its hash — every file `server/data.js` loads but the presentation files `assets`, `local-assets`
(this machine's extracted art) and `emotes`, and not `data/i18n/` — so any code or data change yields a new `cs` and a
machine's own art does not. `plugins` is `[]` and `settings` `{}` for the vanilla content. `canonical` sorts keys and
drops white space; the bytes are the served bytes (a patched file, P1b: the JSON text served from memory), so a browser
hashes what it received. `shared/sha256.js` is SHA-256 in plain JavaScript: the same digests in Node and every browser,
also where `crypto.subtle` is missing (a LAN address is no secure context); the server hashes file bytes with
node:crypto and the tests check that both agree. `.gitattributes` already checks code and data out with LF, so a
Windows clone has the same `cs`. The server computes it once at start (`server/plugins/identity.js`; like the build
tag, a file changed afterwards counts from the next start) and logs `[content] vanilla <16 hex>`.

**Where it shows** (normative: design/network.md §8.4): `welcome.content { vanilla }`; `room.state.content { cs,
vanilla: true, plugins: [] }` — always the vanilla set in P0; the lobby hands `cs` to the match (`opts.cs`, the MATCH
INTERFACE), which copies it into every BattleSpec (`buildBattleSpec` keeps a well-formed `cs`, drops anything else;
`createBattleFromSpec` never reads it — a spec built without one is what it always was); `b.result.cs` is optional and
checked for its form only (the comparison is P1c's); `/healthz content { vanilla, sets: 0 }`; `GET
/cs/<16 hex>/index.json` — the manifest of the content set (every code file's hash, every data file's URL with `?v=`,
hash and size), cached for good, 404 for any other `cs`; the diagnostics' second line `content: vanilla cs=<64 hex>`
(`public/js/diag.js contentLine`; the client keeps `welcome.content` in its store; a plugin room will read `MODDED cs=…
plugins=id@version(hash)…`). The player guide's diagnostics paragraph names the line.

**The registry.** `server/plugins/registry.js` scans the language folders, `packs/` and `plugins/` and answers
`/packs/index.json` exactly as before (the language menu is unchanged), `/packs/<id>/<file>` (only what a lang
manifest names) and the new catalogue `GET /plugins/index.json` (`{ version, app, api, content: { vanilla }, plugins }`,
never cached: every plugin and pack by class with its status, the `unofficial` mark on every class but `lang`, public
manifest fields, size, file count — never a server path). `server/packs.js` is a compatibility export of it;
`shared/packs.js` keeps the language-pack index and forwards the manifest helpers (`PACK_TYPES` now carries each
type's plugin class: `assets` → `cosmetic`, `data` → `content`). Only the class `lang` loads in P0 (a lang plugin of
`plugins/` is a language entry like a pack folder in the live server's index; `tools/packs.mjs` — whose static index
could not serve a plugin's files — and `tools/i18n.mjs` keep reading the language folders and `packs/` only);
`cosmetic` and `content` plugins are checked and listed as `planned`; `script`, `ui` and `trusted` as `unsupported`,
never loaded. Folders starting with `_` (templates) and files at the top of `plugins/` are never read; a duplicate id
(across `packs/` and `plugins/`, any case) or language, a broken manifest, a disallowed file or a link inside a folder
keeps an entry out with a reason; a missing or out-of-range `requires` is a warning (P1c refuses to enable it).

**Manifest 2** (`shared/plugins/manifest.js`; reference PLUGINS.md §3–§5): `manifest: 2`, the id from the folder
(`^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]{1,31}$`), localized name / description, semver `version`, `app` range and `api` level (both
required but for `lang`; `PLUGIN_API = { current: 1, min: 1 }` in `shared/constants.js`), `class`, `scope` by class,
dependencies and ordering hints, typed settings (no free text), permissions that must match the parts, the parts
(`assets`, `theme`, `data`, `operators`, `enemies`, `modes`, `stages`, `dsl`, `i18n`) and entry points by class; the
file rules by class (no SVG, code only in code classes, documents never served) and the limits. Manifest 1 is read
exactly as in 0.2.0 (`readPackManifest`; `normalizeManifest` forwards to it).

**Tools, git, packages.** `node tools/plugin.mjs list` (by class, the skipped and why, the vanilla `cs`) and `check`
(manifest, files, paths, every JSON file's validity, nesting and numbers; exit 1 on a problem, `--strict` on a
warning). `.gitignore` ignores `plugins/*` but `plugins/README.md` and `plugins/_template/` (the templates come with
P1: each class's inner formats are P1a's and P1b's); the release zips ship the tracked ones (`tools/package.mjs`
`RUNTIME_DIRS`), never a server's own plugins; an update never touches them. An entry that cannot be read at all (say,
JSON nested too deep to quote back) is skipped with its reason and never stops the scan or the server's start.

[ASSUMED] — the study's own assumptions and the choices of this step, for the owner to confirm or change:

- every limit (8 MB per JSON file, nesting 64, numbers ±1e9, 200 MB and 5000 files per plugin) and `PLUGIN_API`;
- the id rule, and that `app` and `api` are required for every class but `lang`, `version` must be semver, and a
  numeric setting needs `min` and `max`;
- the presentation files left out of `cs` (`assets`, `local-assets`, `emotes`) and that every other `data/*.json`
  counts (a new gameplay file is included without a code change);
- 16 hex digits in URLs, 8 in the interface, 64 in reports; `/healthz content.sets` counts the modded sets in use;
- the registry checks manifests, file types, sizes and paths whenever it scans, but parses the JSON contents only in
  `tools/plugin.mjs check` (the content set builder of P1b parses and validates what it applies);
- a lang plugin may live in `plugins/` (manifest 2) as well as in `packs/` (manifest 1);
- a symbolic link inside a plugin folder keeps it out (a plugin folder that is itself a link is fine); dot files are
  ignored and never served.

Tests: `test/plugins/manifest.test.js`, `test/plugins/registry.test.js`, `test/plugins/identity.test.js`;
`test/diag.test.js` (the content line); still green: `test/i18n-packs.test.js`, `test/check-imports.test.js`, the
golden results. Normative lines rewritten: design/network.md §8.1 (welcome / room.state additions), §8.4 (new), §14
(BattleSpec `cs?`, `b.result { …, cs? }`).

### 32.3 P1a — cosmetic plugins

Seen: the design study's §3.1, §4.5, §6.1–§6.3, §7.1, §7.4, §7.5, §9.4 and its "P1a 外观" list. A cosmetic plugin changes
what the game shows and plays — avatars, portraits, Spine models, icons, interface pictures, voices, sound effects,
music — never a battle (the simulation reads data, not art), so a room's `cs` stays the vanilla one whatever its art.
With no plugin switched on nothing changes: the room's content is exactly P0's `{ cs, vanilla: true, plugins: [] }`, the
served manifests are byte for byte those of 0.2.6, and the golden results are unchanged (no scenario moved). The
reference is PLUGINS.md §9.

**The overlay** (`shared/plugins/visual.js`): the manifest's `assets` part, inline or a `.json` file, in the shape of
`data/assets.json` — the record containers `chars` (`avatar`, `avatarE2`, `portrait`, `portraitE2`, `spine.front`,
`spine.back`), `enemies` (`icon`, `spine`), `tokens` (`avatar`, `spine`), `bonds` / `items` / `bands` / `skills` /
`modules` / `ui` and `prof.*` (an image), `audio.bgm` (the named tracks) / `audio.bossBgm` (a track `{ loop, intro? }`,
written whole), `audio.voice` / `voiceJp` (a file or a list per slot), `audio.sfx.ui` / `battle` / `units`. A value is a path inside the
plugin folder of the field's kind (`.png .jpg .webp`, `.mp3 .ogg`), a Spine model `{ skel, atlas, textures, pma }` (the
atlas next to the skeleton with its name, the pages inside its folder) or a reference `{ "ref": "<key>" }` to the entry
of the same container in the installation's manifest — a whole record (replaced as a whole; keys beside the `ref` change
fields of it) or one field. A missing entry is a problem; another section or field is a warning and left out; nothing
but URLs and a model's clip data is ever written into the manifest. A field a plugin does not write keeps the
installation's value; a track is written whole (no installation intro before a plugin's loop); a model written for an
enemy or a token takes away its local-client model `spineLocal` (the client draws that one first) — an implied write,
never reported as an override of its own. A write to an entry the installation has needs
`assets.override`, a new entry `assets.add`. The overlay becomes a list of writes (`ops`) in the plugin's order;
resolved, a file is `/p/<id>/<8 hex>/<path>` (the first 8 hex digits of its SHA-256 — of its model's files' SHA-256s for
every file of a Spine model, so pixi-spine finds the atlas and pages beside the skeleton), a reference the entry's value
in the manifest it is resolved against (the served one). `applyOps` writes each value whole, copying the objects on the
way; `mergePluginOps` keeps the load order — the later plugin wins — and reports every path two plugins change (the
same, or one inside the other: `override { path, plugins }`). `theme` is not applied (a warning); fonts are not part of
the overlay.

**The load order** (`shared/plugins/order.js`, the study's §4.8): a plugin's `requires` join the set (`auto`) when they
may (installed, loaded, of the class and scope, in range) — else PLUGIN_DEPENDENCY; a `conflicts` in range with another
plugin of the set is PLUGIN_CONFLICT; requires / optional / loadAfter put a plugin after, loadBefore before; among the
plugins free to go, the higher `priority`, then the lower id; a cycle is PLUGIN_DEPENDENCY.

**Spine models**: their clip roles, durations, events, hit times and bounds come from `plugin.lock.json`, written by
`node tools/plugin.mjs pack <dir>`, which parses each skeleton the way `tools/fetch-assets.mjs` parses the game's
(`tools/assets/skel.mjs`, `anim-roles.mjs`; an operator's three skill slots, an enemy's numbered skills — both for a
skeleton both use), checks the atlas pages against `textures`, and adds every file's SHA-256 and size; an overlay
problem (a reference the manifest lacks) leaves the lock unwritten. A lock made from another skeleton (its SHA-256) is a
problem. The server rebuilds the lock's clip data in the shape the tool writes (`readPluginLock`: the known roles, every
clip they play an animation of the skeleton, ≤ 256 animations and 64 events, names ≤ 64 characters, durations and hit
times ≤ 600 s, bounds as four numbers; depth ≤ 64; maps without a prototype), so nothing a hand-made lock adds reaches a
browser (review of 2026-10-12: a hand-made lock used to pass any role, name or nesting through to every client, and a
deeply nested one made the room's documents fail). `tools/assets/skel.mjs parseSkel` copies a buffer that is a view into a larger one first (the Spine runtime
reads `.buffer` from its start; a small file read with `fs.readFileSync` comes from Node's pool) — the game's own models
are read as before.

**The registry** (`server/plugins/registry.js`) loads the class `cosmetic` now (`status: 'loaded'`; the pack view's
`supported` stays the lang class): when it scans it reads a cosmetic plugin's overlay and lock, checks them against this
installation's `data/assets.json` (read only when a cosmetic plugin needs it), counts what its references copy out of it
(≤ 2 MB), and hashes the files that shape it (SHA-256, kept by path + size + mtime across scans); a problem keeps the
plugin out like any other. Its folder signature now looks six levels deep (a model's files sit at
`spine/<model>/front/…`) and includes `data/assets.json`'s size and time (a manifest rewritten while the server runs is
read again). `pluginFile(id, path)` is the whitelist of `/p/`; `rescan()` reads the folders again however
they look. A catalogue entry of a loaded cosmetic plugin adds `vs` (the set of it alone) and `artBytes`.

**The visual hash** `vs = sha256(canonical({ v: 2, assets: <SHA-256 of data/assets.json>, overlays: [{ id, version, files,
writes }] }))` (the study's §6.1), `files` mapping every file that shapes a plugin's overlay — `plugin.json`, the lock, the
`assets` file, every file the overlay serves — to its SHA-256, `writes` the SHA-256 of the plugin's writes as served
(round 3, §32.3.2: `vs` pins which file goes to which slot).

**The visual sets** (`server/plugins/visualSet.js`): `resolve(ids, { scope })` — loaded cosmetic plugins of the scope,
closed and sorted, ≤ 300 MB of art and ≤ 4 MB copied by references (PLUGIN_UNKNOWN / PLUGIN_BLOCKED / PLUGIN_DEPENDENCY /
PLUGIN_CONFLICT / PLUGIN_LIMIT otherwise) — names the set by its `vs` and keeps it: a room holds its set while it uses it
(`hold` / `release`, never dropped meanwhile), of the others the 256 most recently used stay (a plugin's own set is known
from the catalogue). Its document: `GET /vs/<vs16>/overlay.json` — `{ visual: { v, vs, assets, overlays }, writes: [{ id,
ops }] }`, the composition and each plugin's writes in its own terms (paths of its folder, references, a model's clip
data), never a URL nor a copy of the art manifest; `no-cache` with the body's hash as ETag (the last 8 built are kept; a
document is built once however many ask). `GET /p/<id>/<hash8>/<path>` (`server/http/static.js`) serves only a file a loaded cosmetic
plugin's overlay names — not `plugin.json`, the lock, a document or another file of the folder, no dot file, no `..`, no
real path out of the folder (checked again when served: a link put in later is not followed) —, `immutable` at the hash
of its bytes (its own, or its model's), the current file with `no-cache` at any other (as `/assets/~<hash>/` does); a
file found changed has the registry read the folders again (at most every 5 s). In the traffic counters `/p/` counts as
assets (audio for `.mp3` / `.ogg`), `/vs/` as data.

**The room** (`server/lobby.js`, design/network.md §8.4): `room.setPlugins { plugins: [{ id, settings? }] }` — host-only,
before the match (ROOM_STARTED), in the heavy rate-limit bucket (`server/net.js HEAVY_TYPES`), and like a difficulty change
it un-readies the other humans; the same set again changes nothing, and when only its 「自动」 marks change (a plugin first
switched on automatically, now chosen) the state is broadcast without un-readying. The room holds its set in the visual
sets until it changes or the room goes. The room's content becomes `{ cs, vanilla: true, vs, plugins: [{ id, version, class: 'cosmetic', hash, auto? }],
warnings, bytes }`: `cs` stays the vanilla one, so the match receives the vanilla `cs` and its BattleSpecs are those of
the original content; an empty list brings back exactly the vanilla shape. The set is the room's: kept across its matches
and a host change. `settings` is read by the classes that have settings (none of a cosmetic plugin). New ERR codes, in
every language pack: PLUGIN_UNKNOWN, PLUGIN_BLOCKED, PLUGIN_DEPENDENCY, PLUGIN_CONFLICT, PLUGIN_LIMIT.

**The browser**: `public/js/content.js` follows `room.state.content.vs` — it fetches `/vs/<vs16>/overlay.json` as text,
parses it, checks that its composition hashes to the room's `vs` (`checkVisualDoc`) and makes it the room layer
(`public/js/assets.js` cosmetic layers: the installation's manifest → the room's set → the player's personal plugins),
composed on the manifest the page loaded itself: every plugin URL built from the composition's file table, every
reference copied out of the page's manifest, a write that does not fit left out (`shared/plugins/visual.js`
prepareVisualDoc / layVisualOps — review round 2, §32.3.1); leaving the room, or a room without plugins, takes the layer
away — one room's art never stays behind or piles up. data.js shows `assets` through the layers (`setView`), so every `data.get('assets')` of the interface, the audio and
the asset store the renderer uses (seeded with the manifest as downloaded, laying the layers over it itself) draw the
room's art; a layer change is told like a late manifest. A document that fails or will not download (two retries) leaves
the original art and says so once (a toast; an attempt is given up after 30 s). The room's top bar shows 「原版」 or
「插件 ×N」, which opens the 插件 panel (`public/js/ui/pluginPanel.js`: the server's cosmetic plugins of scope `room` with
the host's switches — read-only for the others and during a match —, versions, authors, sizes, 非官方, 「自动」, a note for
one made for other game versions, the override warnings; a room plugin the server no longer offers stays by its id, its
switch only turning it off, and is never sent again). 设置 → 外观插件 switches the server's cosmetic plugins of scope `personal` for this browser only
(`localStorage` `sp.pref.cosmetics`): each is fetched as `/vs/<vs16>/overlay.json` of its catalogue `vs` (side by side),
checked, and laid over whatever the page draws. The layers keep the manifest they make per base object (data.js's copy
and the renderer's store's may be two). The title screen says 「本服务器安装了 N 个插件」 when the catalogue lists unofficial
plugins (a button: their list, what this version does with each). The diagnostics' content line: `vanilla cs=… cosmetic
vs=… plugins=id@version(hash),…` in a room with cosmetic plugins, ` personal=id,…` for the player's own.

**Template and tools**: `plugins/_template/cosmetic/` (references only — no file of the game; the folder name is the id);
`node tools/plugin.mjs pack <dir>` (above); `check` follows a cosmetic plugin's references to the checkout's
`data/assets.json`.

**Statistics** (the owner's decision of 2026-10-13): a match whose plugins are all cosmetic plays the vanilla `cs` and
counts as the original game in the statistics overview (`room.state.content.vanilla` stays `true`) — its gameplay and
numbers are the original ones, and a player's personal cosmetics never matter; only matches with plugins that change
gameplay are left out (with P1b / P1c). Every stats record names the room's `vs` (round 2).

[ASSUMED] — the choices of this step, for the owner to confirm or change:

- a reference names the installation's own entry, never another plugin's, and an entry of the same container only;
- a field a plugin does not write keeps the installation's value, a BGM track is written whole, and a model written for an
  enemy or a token takes away its local-client model;
- a Spine model's clip data comes only from `plugin.lock.json` (never written by hand); the server never parses a
  skeleton; the lock's file list is informational — the server hashes the files itself;
- the `files` of a plugin's part of `vs`: `plugin.json`, the lock, the `assets` file and every file the overlay serves;
- a cosmetic plugin's overlay is read and its files hashed when the registry scans (synchronously; a 200 MB plugin about a
  second once), and a problem keeps it out;
- the visual set documents answer `no-cache` with the body's hash as ETag, not `immutable`; a browser checks their
  composition and composes the art itself (every plugin URL from the composition's file table, references out of its
  own manifest — round 2: it never adopts a manifest the server put together);
- `/p/` at a hash that does not name the file's bytes serves the current file with `no-cache` (as `/assets/~<hash>/`),
  not 409 as the study had it; a removed plugin's files are 404;
- limits: 300 MB of art per set, 20 000 writes per plugin, 16 files per voice slot, 32 override warnings per set, 2 MB
  copied by references per plugin and 4 MB per set, the lock's clip data (256 animations, 64 events, names ≤ 64
  characters, ≤ 600 s), the last 256 sets no room holds and 8 documents kept, 30 s per attempt at a set's document;
- when only a room's 「自动」 marks change, the state is broadcast without un-readying;
- personal plugins apply after the room's set (round 2: ordered like a room's set — requirements, conflicts, load
  order); a choice that went bad since keeps what still fits, by id; one the server lacks stays switched on in the
  browser;
- `theme` and fonts are not applied yet (a warning); the title screen counts every unofficial plugin the server has, of
  any class (round 2: the count comes with `/healthz` and `welcome`, the catalogue only when needed; round 3: a page of a
  server without plugins loads three small modules more than 0.2.6's at start — `public/js/content.js`,
  `public/js/cosmetics.js`, `public/js/ui/pluginTag.js` —, the panel, what composes a set, the load-order function and
  the asset store each only when needed);
- the error texts and the panel's wording (PLUGIN_*, 「原版」, 「插件 ×N」, 「外观插件」).

Tests: `test/plugins/visual.test.js`, `test/plugins/order.test.js`, `test/plugins/visualSet.test.js`,
`test/plugins/tool.test.js`, `test/lobby-plugins.test.js`, `test/ui/plugins-client.test.js`; `test/plugins/manifest.test.js`
and `test/plugins/registry.test.js` (cosmetic loaded), `test/diag.test.js` (the content line), `test/asset-paths.test.js`
(the traffic categories); the browser test `test/ui/plugins.e2e.test.js` (`SP_E2E=1`). Art in the tests is made on the
spot (`test/helpers/pluginFixtures.js`: PNGs, a minimal Spine 3.8 skeleton the client's parser reads). Normative lines
rewritten: design/network.md §8.4 (room.setPlugins, room.state.content, the catalogue's `vs`, `/vs/`, `/p/`, the PLUGIN_*
errors).

### 32.3.1 P1a — review round 2

An independent review (2026-10-12) found what P1a had left loose; the changes, each a decision of this step:

- **The browser composes the art itself** (it was handed a manifest the server put together): `/vs/<vs16>/overlay.json`
  carries the set's composition and each plugin's writes in the plugin's own terms (paths of its folder, references, a
  model's clip data) and no URL; `/vs/<vs16>/assets.json` is gone. The page builds every plugin URL from the
  composition's file table — the very table `vs` hashes —, copies every reference out of the manifest it loaded itself
  (`/data/assets.json`, 0.2.6's `/assets/~<hash>/` URLs), lays the plugins in the composition's load order, and leaves
  out what does not fit: a file the table lacks or of another kind than its field, a path outside the containers, a
  model out of its folder or of the lock's shape, a reference its manifest lacks (`shared/plugins/visual.js`
  prepareVisualDoc / layVisualOps; one toast per page). What it draws is exactly what `vs` names, and a room's set costs
  a few KB instead of the 1.3 MB manifest. (The point is consistency and bandwidth, not defence against the server.)
- **`/p/` serves only the very file the scan hashed**: the request `lstat`s the path — a symbolic link put in place of a
  served file after the scan is refused (404) wherever it leads, another file or a dot file of the folder included —
  and wants the real path the scan recorded (a link put in place of a folder on its way is refused too).
- **A file is the hashed one while it keeps its size, mtime, inode and ctime** (`registry.js statIdentity`): one rewritten
  with its old size and mtime put back (`rsync -t`, `touch -r`) is no longer served `immutable` under its old hash —
  `no-cache`, and the registry reads the folders again and hashes it again (its hash cache keys on all four).
- **`room.setPlugins` drops the host's result replay only when the set changed** — its `vs` or its 「自动」 marks: the
  same set again, or a refused one, leaves a settlement screen that is still open.
- **A plugin's audio is fetched without its extension**, like the installation's through `/media/` (download managers
  pop up a dialog for every fetch ending in `.mp3`): `/p/<id>/<hash8>/<path without .mp3 / .ogg>` serves the listed
  audio file of that name — of a `.mp3` and a `.ogg` of one name, the one whose hash the URL names — with the same
  whitelist and cache rules (`server/http/static.js`); `public/js/media.js mediaUrl` maps a plugin's audio URL to it and
  `public/js/audio.js` keeps its fallback to the URL with the extension. The traffic counters count it as audio.
- **A plugin file that will not load falls back, per entry**: an `<img>` of the interface (one capture listener for the
  page, `public/js/content.js`), a picture or a Spine model of the board (`public/js/assets.js` reports its own failures
  — a model not while the tab is hidden: a load the hidden tab left hanging is not the file's fault, GitHub #68), the
  shadow sprite, a sound (`public/js/audio.js` onFailed, wired in `main.js`) → the cosmetic layers leave the writes with
  that URL out (`createCosmeticLayers fail`), so the entry draws the art beneath it — the installation's, or the room's
  under a personal plugin — for the rest of the page's life [ASSUMED], the views re-resolve it (above), and a sound plays
  the installation's entry at once (the BGM track, the voice line of the same slot, the same UI / battle / unit sound).
  The page says it once (a toast).
- **A changed plugin is the host's to apply again**: after every rescan of the registry (`registry.onScan`) the visual
  sets look at the sets rooms hold; one whose plugin changed (its composition hash) or is gone is told once
  (`visualSet.onStale`), and the lobby sends that room's host an `m.toast` (during a match, once the room is back in its
  lobby, when the panel can change them) to apply the plugins again; the panel's 「重新应用」 (the host, a room with
  plugins) sends the same choice, which makes the set of the files as they are now. Nothing changes by itself: until then the room keeps its set (its changed files under the old URLs
  with `no-cache`, what will not load falling back).
- **A page of a server without plugins never asks for the catalogue**: the server says how many unofficial plugins it
  has as `content.plugins` in `/healthz` (the build guard's first check reads it at once — the title screen's count
  before any hello: `public/js/ui/buildGuard.js` onHealth) and in `welcome` (a reconnect), only when there are some, so
  a server without plugins sends exactly what it sent before; the page keeps it as the store's `installedPlugins` and
  fetches `/plugins/index.json` only when it is > 0 and something needs it — the title's list, the 插件 panel, 设置's
  外观插件, a switched-on personal plugin.
- **A player's personal plugins form a set like a room's** (they were laid in the catalogue's order, requirements and
  conflicts ignored): the catalogue now lists what the load order reads of a plugin (`requires`, `optional`,
  `conflicts`, `loadAfter`, `loadBefore`, `priority`), and the browser runs the room's function on the switched-on ones
  (`public/js/content.js` personalSet → `shared/plugins/order.js`): a plugin another needs is on as 「自动」, a switch
  that would make a conflict or needs a plugin the server lacks is refused with the room's message (PLUGIN_CONFLICT /
  PLUGIN_DEPENDENCY), and a choice that went bad since (the server's plugins changed) keeps what still fits, by id, the
  others shown as not in effect [ASSUMED].
- **Statistics record the room's art**: every stats record — settled or quit — names the room's visual set `vs`
  (room.state.content.vs; null without cosmetic plugins, also when compacted; a player's personal plugins are not the
  room's and are not recorded; an id-less record's content id leaves it out, so a record from before 0.3.0 keeps its
  id). The overview does not filter on it: a match with only cosmetic plugins counts as the original game — the owner's
  decision of 2026-10-13 (its gameplay and numbers are unchanged; personal cosmetics never matter); only matches with
  plugins that change gameplay are left out, with P1b / P1c.
- **Two things a cosmetic plugin does not change, written down**: the split battle music `audio.bgm.combatAlts` (the
  mode's own 开战 tracks, 骑士之日 / 无畏者, fixed per round — the client plays them instead of `combat`, so a plugin's
  `combat` is heard only where none plays); and replays and BattleSpecs carry `cs` only, not `vs` (P1c): a battle replayed
  from a spec or a bug report shows the installation's art — the report's content line names the set it had.
- **A second look at this round** (an independent review of these commits) fixed: the real path a scan records is read
  with the native realpath — the one a request compares with — so a plugins folder spelled in another case than on disk
  (macOS, Windows) is no longer refused; the compressed-body cache keys on the inode and ctime too, and a changed plugin
  file's ETag is its stat identity, so a rewrite that kept its size and mtime never comes out compressed with the old
  bytes nor revalidates as them; an implied `spineLocal` clear goes with its model write (left out when the write is
  dropped, fails to load or its reference is missing — the entry keeps its local model); the load-order function is
  loaded with the catalogue, not at boot; a late local-client manifest leaves a shown model alone, as before; the host's
  notice waits for the end of a match; an upper-case audio extension is not stripped; a plugin model the renderer cannot
  build falls back like one that did not load; and the music follows the art (`audio.js refreshBgm`: the screen's track
  again when the cosmetic layers change its URLs). Not changed: between the `lstat` / realpath check and the open of the
  file a link could still be swapped in (it needs write access to the plugin folder) [ASSUMED: accepted].
- Tests of this round: `test/plugins/visual.test.js` and `visualSet.test.js` (the composition, the document, `/p/`'s
  links, stat identity and extension-less audio), `test/lobby-plugins.test.js`, `test/ui/plugins-client.test.js`,
  `test/ui/plugins-audio.test.js`, `test/render/cosmetic-refresh.test.js`, `test/ui/stats.test.js`,
  `test/media-url.test.js`, `test/ui/buildGuard.test.js`, `test/asset-paths.test.js`, and the browser test
  `test/ui/plugins.e2e.test.js` (three cases now; the fast server takes a plugins folder: `SP_PLUGINS_DIR`).
- **What is already on the board follows the art** (P1a had views built afterwards draw the new art and a model on the
  board keep its own — but a room's set arriving after the board was drawn, a reconnect or a spectator joining a match,
  left the first models for the whole match): `render/units.js` retryAssets — called on every `assets.onChange` — loads
  a picture or a Spine model the manifest names now when it is not the one shown or loading (compared by URL; the new
  model replaces the old in place like a Front ⇄ Back swap, a load in flight of the old one is superseded, a replaced
  picture stays on the diamond until the new one is there — a drawn diamond is cached by its picture's URL too);
  `render/app.js` asks the backdrop mountains and the shadow sprite again whenever their `ui` URL changes (none named any
  more: none drawn, the procedural shadow). Tested with the fake PIXI (`test/render/cosmetic-refresh.test.js`) and in the
  browser (a personal plugin switched during a match changes the picture of the piece already on the board).

### 32.3.2 P1a — review round 3

A second review of round 2 (2026-10-13) confirmed its fixes and found what was left:

- **`vs` pins the writes** (round 2's document carried each plugin's ops beside the composition, but `vs` hashed only the
  file tables — the same `vs` could put another file of the table into a slot): each plugin's part of a `vs` now has
  `writes`, the SHA-256 of the canonical JSON of its ops as the server keeps and serves them (`writesHashOf`: slots,
  file names, references, clip data — never a URL; computed when the registry scans the plugin), and the composition's
  version is 2. A browser hashes the ops of the document again (`checkVisualDoc`): ops that are not the ones `vs`
  names, a plugin listed twice or not in the composition, or anything that is no plain data refuse the document through
  the usual path — `fetchVisualDoc` `{ error }`, no room layer, one toast; nothing throws.
- **Art a plugin alone gives leaves the board with its layer** (round 2 replaced a picture or model only by another one):
  when the manifest names no picture any more, `retryAssets` gives the view a fresh picture record (a load in flight
  lands in the old one) and takes the image off the diamond; when it names no model, `_dropModel` supersedes a load in
  flight (`_spineReq`: it releases its entry when it lands), drops and releases the model shown and shows the diamond —
  a token or an enemy the installation draws as a plain diamond, skinned by a plugin, is a plain diamond again when the
  plugin goes (`test/render/cosmetic-refresh.test.js`: both, and the layer taken away mid-load).
- **The folders' own check sees a quiet rewrite**: the registry's signature of `plugins/` keeps the whole stat identity
  of each file (size, mtime, inode, ctime — `statIdentity`), so a file rewritten with its old size and mtime put back is
  read again on the next look (at most a second later), not only once a page asks for it at `/p/`; the hosts of the
  rooms using it hear of it then (onStale).
- **The bond cue falls back like the other sounds**: `_playBondCue` replays the cue the manifest names once a plugin's
  cue failed (`fellBack`), as `_play` does.
- **A lean title page** (this week's other goal is the host's bandwidth): the cosmetic layers moved into a small module of
  their own (`public/js/cosmetics.js`, which loads `shared/plugins/visual.js` only with the first set — `loadVisual`;
  content.js loads it before it fetches a document), the room's tag and the facts the screens show into
  `public/js/ui/pluginTag.js`, and the plugin file extensions into `shared/plugins/manifest.js` (the audio code needs
  them at start); `public/js/assets.js` loads with the first match again, as in 0.2.6, and `public/js/ui/pluginPanel.js`
  when the room's panel or the title's list opens. A page of a server without plugins now loads three small modules more
  than 0.2.6 at start (content.js, cosmetics.js, ui/pluginTag.js) and none of the panel, the asset store or what composes
  a set: a check of `main.js`'s import graph (`test/ui/plugins-client.test.js`) and of the requests a vanilla page makes
  (`test/ui/plugins.e2e.test.js`) keep it so.

### 32.4 Open points for P1

- P1a leftovers: the theme property allowlist and plugin fonts; a zip from `tools/plugin.mjs pack` (done: §32.9). (A
  room's set whose plugin changes on disk: round 2 tells the host, who re-applies — §32.3.1; the studio's rooms apply it
  by themselves — §32.9.)
- P1b is implemented in §32.5; room integration (P1c) in §32.6, which keeps its own open points.
- P1c (done, §32.6): `room.setPlugins` / `room.contentReady`, the routes consuming C1, the worker host and the async
  lobby, the client fetching `/cs/<cs>/` as bytes (hash before decoding: a BOM would be lost by `res.text()`), the
  b.result `cs` check, the statistics `content` field (the `vs` is recorded since P1a round 2; the overview leaves out a
  match with gameplay plugins — one with cosmetic plugins only counts as vanilla, the owner's decision of 2026-10-13), a
  BattleSpec's optional `vs` (replays and diagnostics showing the art they had — the sim never reads
  it), the bug template, the unofficial marks, the server policy file.

### 32.5 P1b — data plugins

The owner's 2026-10-13 release plan, interface C1/C2, and the adopted plugin design's §4 and §6 define this step.
`shared/plugins/patch.js` applies pure atomic patches; `records.js` expands compact operators (shop pairs and/or DIY
unit forms), enemies, waves, stages, modes and reused items/bands/garrisons/choice effects. IDs are namespaced;
existing records are patched and cannot be deleted. Dependencies authorize cross-plugin record changes. No third-party
code executes. The full format and API are PLUGINS.md §10; `schemas/records.schema.json` mirrors the runtime schema.

`validate.js` contains build-data's count-independent reference checks with the same diagnostic text. The builder keeps
official counts, deliberate trigger deviations, source-specific item/token assertions and the official language rule.
Content sets additionally check required fields, numeric types, finite values, paired forms, default-skill equality,
kit/effect refs and nested JSON safety. One wave, and each phase of a branch, may contain at most 64 spawn rows
(no action, or action `SPAWN`), each with an integer count from 1 to 600, and the sum of those counts may not exceed
600 (`PLUGIN_DATA_LIMIT` / `PLUGIN_DATA_REQUIRED`, `shared/plugins/limits.js`). Enemy blackboard keys named `times` or
ending in `times` — skills, talents, a plain `talent` object, abilities, and the same fields on wave overrides — stay
at or below 100. `bb.count` on `global_special_choice_gain_equip` and `single_special_choice_gain_bond_chess` stays at
or below 32. Those specialized caps stay. When `validateData` is given the official tables as `vanilla` (the content-set
path always passes them), every numeric blackboard value under chess, backups, enemies, bands, bonds, effects, items,
waves, choices, garrisons and tokens must satisfy `|v| ≤ max(10 × officialMax[key], 64)` (`PLUGIN_DATA_BOUND`,
`shared/plugins/officialBounds.js`). Flat `bb` objects and `{key,value}` blackboard arrays both count, including a key
that contains `.` or `@`. A key the official tables never have uses 64. The same formula covers the non-blackboard
fields that drive loops: enemy `sp.maxSp`, `sp.initSp` and `sp.increment`, and skill `spCost`, `initSp`, `maxChargeTime`
and `increment` on chess, backup forms and enemies. Ammo and charge counts that live on a blackboard are the keys
`ammo` and `charge`. The message names the key or field, the value, the cap and the official maximum, or that the
official tables have no such key. Official data produces none. `getData` caches the same table, and the loops named
below read it at use time, so a kit built from an unchecked record still stops. Before that table is installed the
clamp falls back to 64. Every content set also carries `bounds` (`exportOfficialBounds`, `v: 1`) computed from the
vanilla tables, never from the composed plugin data. A selection that includes plugins serves the same object as
`bounds/official.json`. That file is not an input of `cs`. `index.bounds.official` names it. A vanilla selection keeps
`bounds` on the set and does not add the file. Integration (with §32.6): the page loads `bounds/official.json` with the
set's data files, checked by its bytes against the manifest (`public/js/content.js` `loadContentSet`), and the battle
runner installs it after `setSimData` (`loadBrowserSim({ data, cs, bounds })`); the match host gets the same object in
its boot message and installs it after `setData` (`server/match/host/entry.js`) — one table on both sides, so a modded
battle simulates with the same caps in the page and in the host that verifies or takes it over. A set that lists no
snapshot runs on the floor (64), never on a scan of its own tables; the original content scans the data it loaded (no
request). `branchSpawn` stops at `MAX_ALIVE_ENEMIES`
(600, locked equal to `PLUGIN_SPAWN_TOTAL`) and does not record one error per refused spawn. The named enemy `times`
loops and those two card-grant loops clamp with the same constants, without flooring a fractional count. `null` is its
own type: a `set` may write it only onto a field that is already `null`, and washing a number through `null` into an
object is `PLUGIN_PATCH_TYPE` on the original data. An enemy `skills` value must be an array and `talents` a plain
object (`PLUGIN_DATA_TYPE`). `talentMods` beside `kit.ref` / `kitRef` is `PLUGIN_SKILL_SPEC`. Plugin ids whose
namespaces collide after `-` becomes `_` (`data` with `data-demo`) are `PLUGIN_RECORD_ID` when the selection is
composed; a folder scanned alone is not refused, and `ab` does not collide with `abc`. A plugin operator `char_x_`
with neither `potDown` nor a talent `potBelow` accepts only potential 6. Official operators stay as they are,
including `char_616_pithst`. 调配 and 自选 omit the potential control for that plugin operator; 练度 stays. A stored
potential the control does not show does not mark the card 已调整. Sync still drops a non-default value and the server
rejects the raw message. No new interface string was added. `server/sim/groundPaths.js` is the shared path
calculation; official output stays byte-identical. A stage whose own JSON changed gets derived paths and deployment
tiles in memory. A content plugin that changes only a wave's `routes` or `extraRoutes` recomputes `groundPaths` for
stages listed on modes that use those waves (a round `template` or `bossTemplates`) and keeps that stage's
`deployTiles`. A content selection's `cs` includes the sha256 of each served `i18n/<lang>.json`. Vanilla and
cosmetic-only selections omit that field, so the P0 hash stays bit-identical. `index.json` is not an input, because it
embeds `cs`. `tools/records-schema.mjs` writes `schemas/records.schema.json` from the runtime `RECORDS_SCHEMA`.

The registry loads content JSON snapshots. C1 `buildContentSet({registry, ids})` resolves dependencies/settings, applies
the selected files, validates the final data and returns an immutable set or only `{problems}`. Success includes `cs`,
`vanilla`, `plugins`, `data`, `files`, `modes`, `problems`, plus `index`, `settings`, `warnings` and `kitted`.
`contentSetFiles(set)` gives a Map of route-relative paths to `{body, sha256, bytes}`. Empty sets use exactly P0's hash
and index for the current checkout. Unmodified files retain disk bytes; modified files hash the serialized bytes served.
No lobby, protocol, HTTP content route or UI is introduced here; those consume C1 in P1c.

C2 kit resolution is extension registry → DSL → JSON SkillSpec → kitRef → official → generic. `extensions.js` is empty.
`kitOf` calls `compileDsl` when the chess record carries `dsl`. The content-set builder validates that spec, writes it
onto the record and stamps `pluginId`; the author's spec is not rewritten. A bad spec is `PLUGIN_DSL_*` and refuses
the whole set, so `setupUnitKit` never sees it as a generic kit. JSON SkillSpec validates the
non-function fields of SIM §7.3, including typed permanent `talentMods`; DIY composition preserves those fields.
Items dispatch by `effectRef` while reading concrete parameters; bands retain concrete buffs and use the referenced
identity for fixed-id global effects. Enemies can reuse a specific existing enemy kit. `GameData` overlays mode-level
global keys (economy, DP, timers etc.) without changing vanilla behavior. Translations use i18nData's source hashes,
computed against merged source records, and remain outside the simulation tables. The served `i18n/<lang>.json` bytes
of a content selection are part of `cs`; vanilla `data/i18n/` is not part of the P0 hash.

[ASSUMED] implementation choices beyond the adopted design:

- Prefixes for new tables: item_x, band_x, garrison_x, effect_x, token_x, wave_x, stage_x (the design specifies chess,
  char, enemy, mode and skill prefixes). A compact item is one non-merging record; complete pairs use add patches.
- Omitted operator stats use simple playable defaults (1000 HP, 100 ATK, zero DEF/RES, 1 s interval, 100 ASPD, one block,
  10 DP, 70 s redeploy); explicit form values override them. No official operator is changed.
- A one-skill DIY clone referencing a chess kit uses that kit's default skill. A one-skill DSL kit uses the same
  `bindDefaultSkill` helper (`server/sim/content/index.js`), so a 自选 piece and a 补位 stand-in field `skills[id]`
  and `selectSkillSpec` reports `skillSource: 'skills'`. Shop chess keeps the default `kit.skill` and does not set
  `skillSource`. Multi-skill clones retain explicit `skills[id]`; the default spec is not copied onto every skill.
- JSON SkillSpec caps counts at 100 and ranges at 1024 offsets within ±64; standard plugin JSON limits still apply.
  Those caps are not the only work bounds. [ASSUMED] 64 spawn rows, 600 enemies, enemy `times` 100 and the two grant
  counts at 32 sit above the official maxima (28 rows, a row count of 10, a wave sum of 70, enemy `times` 8, grant
  counts 2) and far below the JSON ±1e9 window. The key `times` across every table reaches 12 on a chess talent, so
  the systematic cap for that key is 120; an enemy value above 100 still reports `PLUGIN_DATA_LIMIT` as well.
- A registry caches 32 recent compositions; a caller's retained set/bytes survive eviction. Room/worker limits are P1c.
  [ASSUMED] 32 is a process-local cache size, not a room limit.
- Served `i18n/<lang>.json` sha256s enter a content selection's `cs`. Vanilla `data/i18n/` stays out of the P0 hash.
  Declared plugin-file hashes alone do not name the served translation snapshot.
- [ASSUMED] The systematic cap is `max(10 × official absolute maximum, 64)` (`PLUGIN_BOUND_FACTOR` /
  `PLUGIN_BOUND_FLOOR`). Official blackboard objects are flat string-to-number maps; a nested object is read by its
  leaf key. choices, garrisons, tokens and waves are scanned with the tables named above so a higher official value
  is not missed; that only loosens a cap. The pass runs only when `vanilla` is passed. `validateData(data)` without
  it keeps the older checks. Measured official absolute maxima: `times` 12, `attack@times` 5, `count` 20, blackboard
  `sp` 35, enemy `sp.maxSp` 55. The fallback 64 is at or above every official input of the clamped loops, so a fight
  that starts before the table is cached does not change.
- [ASSUMED] Engine clamps use that cap at call time: 左乐 S3 `times`, 郁金香 S3 `times` (the scheduled slash count and
  `lastAt`), 维什戴尔 shock `count` derived from `attack@times` (the `attack@enable_third_attack` branch stays the
  constant 2, then shares the same cap), band `bb.count` loops for
  `preparation_start_gain_chess_from_round`, `preparation_start_gain_chess_every_n_round`,
  `prep_start_gain_chess_from_pool_in_round`, `band_coin_cost_gain_random_char_by_shop_level`,
  `gain_bond_char_per_round`, `round_start_gain_char_chess_in_shop_every_n_round`, the shop fill
  `band_first_self_refresh_present_char`, the level-up item roll `up_shop_add_special_goods`, 铃兰's board slice
  `round_start_activate_char_chess_effect_in_board`, and `refresh_cnt` / `choice_cnt` on
  `preparation_start_add_special_goods_every_n_round`, plus 维多利亚 `payHammers`, 黑云 `sp.maxSp` and the per-grab
  blackboard `sp`, and 狱警 killer `pierce.bb.times`. Official inputs of these loops are at or below 55.
- [ASSUMED] Durations are not given an engine loop cap. No per-tick empty `for` loop on `duration` was found, and not
  every kit was dynamically scanned. A blackboard `duration` is still a numeric blackboard value, so a plugin that
  raises it past the systematic cap is `PLUGIN_DATA_BOUND`. `cooldown` and `initCooldown` are the same class and are
  not field caps.
- [ASSUMED] Some `count` reads are not loops: `addLayers` is O(1), the bond-layer room stops at 999, and one coin
  settlement multiplies once. They still fail `PLUGIN_DATA_BOUND` when the blackboard number exceeds the cap. They
  are not per-count freezes. Band grant loops and 维多利亚 hammers do iterate `count`; an earlier note that every
  other `bb.count` was a single addition was wrong.
- [ASSUMED] `jsonProblems` still allows a number whose absolute value is exactly 1e9 (`Math.abs(v) > 1e9` is strict).
  A number that is neither a blackboard value nor one of the loop fields above can still hold that value.
- [ASSUMED] `index.json` cannot diverge while the hashed i18n bytes, data hashes, plugins and settings stay the same.
  It is not itself a hash input. A future index field taken from outside those inputs could desync a cache that treats
  the index as a pure function of `cs`.
- [ASSUMED] Direct `new Battle` and test constructors can still pass an uncapped spawn list. The content path goes
  through `validateData`, and `branchSpawn` still stops at the living cap. `_queueSpawn` still caps each queued row,
  not the number of rows. `branchSpawn` still uses `Math.max(1, raw)` for a non-integer count on that direct path;
  plugin validation requires an integer count of at least 1.
- [ASSUMED] Official `char_616_pithst` has no `potDown` and may still set a potential; that stores the number and
  changes no stat. Only a plugin `char_x_` without a curve is refused. The potential control is omitted for that
  operator on 调配 and 自选. The browser was not used; a vnode walk covers the control.
- [ASSUMED] A route-only recompute does not recompute `deployTiles`. Bot traffic estimates then use the new paths;
  deployment lists stay the stage's own. Left unchanged on purpose: changing it would change deployment lists and bots.
- [ASSUMED] Namespace collision is checked when a selection is composed, not when a plugin folder is scanned alone.

Description counts and step lengths (third review). Counts taken out of skill, talent or trait text go through
`textCount` (`shared/plugins/textNumber.js`). The capture must be finite. The cap is `max(10 × the official maximum
of that phrase, 64)`, or 64 when vanilla text never matches the pattern (`TEXT_COUNT_PATTERNS`). A matched run that
is not finite (a string of nines, which becomes `Infinity`) clamps to that cap and does not fall through to the
official fallback. [ASSUMED] Official captures stay at or below the scanned maximum, and every scanned maximum is at
most 15, so the cold floor of 64 does not change a vanilla count either. Percents and durations use `textFinite`:
they stay finite and are not forced down to a count cap. `持续120秒` stays 120. `伤害降低80%` stays 80. `parseN` and
`textNum` are `textFinite`. Measured phrase maxima: `连发` 9 (cap 90), `攻击N次` 15 (cap 150), `偷取触发N次` 2
(cap 64), `连击` 10 (cap 100), `连续攻击N次` 2 (cap 64), `总计N次` 10 (cap 100), `额外攻击N个目标` 2 (cap 64),
`至多N个` 5 (cap 64), `至多N只` 3 (cap 64), `初始N只` 2 (cap 64), `在N个友方单位间跳跃` 3 (cap 64),
`在N个敌人间跳跃` 4 (cap 64), `最多拥有N个` 13 (cap 130), `存在N名` 3 (cap 64).

A subtract loop whose interval, period or per-step cost comes from a plugin number uses `stepOf`
(`shared/plugins/steps.js`). A non-positive value is unchanged, so 0 still means off. A positive value at or above
the official minimum positive of that key is unchanged, even when it is shorter than one tick. Official blackboard
`interval` is 0.03 and `atk_interval` is 0.025. Raising those to one tick would change vanilla combat.
[ASSUMED] `battle.every` already clamps its own scheduler to one tick and runs at most 8 times per tick
(`server/sim/battle/hooks.js`). Subtract loops that already run at the official 0.03 keep that step. Anything
smaller than the official minimum is raised to `max(one tick, official minimum / 10)`. When the table is not
installed, only a step below a tenth of a tick is raised, so a cold cache does not lift 0.03. `STEP_TICK` is `1/30`,
the same value as `TICK`. A per-tick subtract loop also stops after 8 iterations in one call (`STEP_PER_TICK`), the
same budget as `battle.every`. A burst that is not one tick of accumulated time uses `burstLimit`, which is the
systematic cap of the official iteration count, not 8. 娜斯提's skill-end catch-up is `min(burstLimit(8),
floor(duration / step))`. Official duration 4 and interval 0.5 is 8 pulses. A duration of 1e9 still stops at 80.
`duration` is not added to `SKILL_FIELDS`. Validation rejects a positive step key below the official minimum / 10
(or below a tenth of a tick when the key has no official positive) with `PLUGIN_DATA_BOUND` and the text `低于最小步长`.
[ASSUMED] `isStepKey` matches a key that ends in `interval` or `period`, a key that ends in `cost`, and `coin_cnt`.
It does not match `spCost`, `cost_add_max`, `atk_cost` or `runtime_cost`. A legitimate small cost whose name matches
the pattern would be rejected. A value that sits between the official minimum / 10 and the official minimum, and is
also below one tick, passes validation and is raised by the engine once the table is installed.

Author-facing `PLUGIN_DATA_BOUND` text stays Chinese. It is not a player string. `officialBounds.js` carries a
file-level `i18n-ignore-file` mark. The `联防` diagnostic in `validate.js` keeps its line `i18n-ignore`. `errors.js`
belongs to the later catalogue and is not added here.

`textCount`, `stepOf`, `burstLimit`, `boundedCount`, `bbCap` and `fieldCap`.

[ASSUMED] Residual risks left in place:

- `new Battle` and a test that writes a kit field after validation skip `validateData`. The engine clamps above are
  the backstop. `jsonProblems` still allows a number whose absolute value is exactly 1e9 on a field that is neither
  a blackboard value nor one of the loop fields.
- `ai.js` `hits` and `funnelBodyHits`: since the round-3 review (HIGH-A, the 伺夜 wolf pack — `tokens.js` read `至多N只`
  and `初始N只` with a raw regex, and with every attack 0 an attack looped 1e16 times on a target that never fell) the pack
  reads both through `textCount`, and `resolveHit` caps any count it is handed at `PLUGIN_TIMES_MAX` (100) — official
  counts stay at or below 15, so no vanilla attack changes. The loop still ends early when the target dies.
- The pick-one offer (`config.economy.rewardOffer.count`, official 3; round-3 review HIGH-B: 1e9 rolled the pool 4e9 times
  when a piece with 免费特殊招募 was sold, and 1e9 times on a promotion): `GameData.rewardOffer()` caps it at
  `MAX_OFFER_SLOTS` (6, the slots an offer shows — a larger count only rolled for slots nobody saw) and the data check
  refuses a count that is not a whole number from 1 to 6 (`PLUGIN_OFFER_SLOTS`, a test locks the two).
- The loop-cap scan (`test/plugins/loopCaps.test.js`) reads `while (` only; a data-driven `for` is found by review, as
  the two above were.
- Chain heals and chain attacks stop when the next target is missing. They are bounded by the unit list.
- `roundPick` (`match/waves.js` around the weight pair) uses `maxLevelCnt` as a weight, not as an iteration count.
  That weight is not capped. `makeTypeSchedule` is the loop, and it stops at 256.
- `factions` is not in `BOUND_TABLES` (its numbers are not blackboards). Integration added `appendFactionBounds`: every
  number of `factions.generation` (maxLevelCnt, specialEnemyNum, firstHalfMaxRound, the typeSlots, the beFactors, the
  replaced counts …), each type's `count` and `weight` and each entry's `weight` follow the same rule against the
  official `factions` — and the replaced counts also stay within `PLUGIN_ACTION_UNITS` (50, `shared/plugins/limits.js`),
  the clamp `server/match/waves.js` applies, so what passes is what the generator plays (`maxLevelCnt` 1e9 →
  `PLUGIN_DATA_BOUND`, cap 150). The schedule loop still stops at 256.
- `professions.js` bombarder `shockTimes` (`attack@times`, and 迷迭香 `add_times` added on top) schedules one
  `battle.after` per extra hit and is not clamped again in the engine. Validation bounds each blackboard key.
  Official `attack@times` is 5. A direct write of `profile.shockTimes` can still schedule that many callbacks.
- 提丰 ammo and talent stacks still use `Math.floor(num(...))`. Those blackboard numbers are bounded at validation.
  The rain-hit `battle.after` loop is the one capped in the engine.
- 娜仁图亚 `come_back` grows by the capped `cnt`. A direct write of the caught counter can still spin.
- 可露希尔 S1's step is `duration / n`. `stepOf` leaves a result at or above the official `cost` minimum (1) unchanged.
  A computed step below that minimum is raised once the table is installed.
- 黑键 `coin_cnt` uses `int()`, so `1e-18` becomes 0 and the spend does not run. The burst cap is 100000, not 8.
- 狼主 `hp_ratio` stops after 64 crossings of one hit, and stops early when the next threshold does not move.
  Official play crosses once. The 287 golden scenarios still match with that cap.
- Discarding 真言 overflow above `burstLimit(PALSY_MAX)` drops the extra count instead of bouncing it.
- `attack@kill_damage` stays a damage number. It is not a loop count. Its systematic cap is large because the
  official value is large.
- An unregistered description pattern caps at 64. A phrase the scanner is too narrow to see would clamp an official
  count. The registered patterns are the ones in the table. Call sites sometimes use a shorter numeral class than
  the scanner. The scanner is the wider one, so the cap only gets looser.
- The bounds snapshot is the page's copy of the vanilla table. A worker that does not install it keeps the floor of
  64, and a plugin value of 80 would pass validation and then be clamped. This package does not contain that worker.
- The browser was not used. `loadBrowserSim` is covered by the content-battle test and by the bounds-file test.

Tests: `test/plugins/patch.test.js`, `records.test.js`, `contentSet.test.js`, `contentBattle.test.js`,
`reviewLimits.test.js`, `loopCaps.test.js`, `test/match/potential.test.js`, `test/ui/potential-ui.test.js`. These check coded refusals
(spawn budgets, `times`, grant counts, `PLUGIN_DATA_BOUND` on 左乐 / 郁金香 / 维什戴尔, band and 维多利亚 `count`,
黑云 `sp.maxSp` and blackboard `sp`, an invented key at 64, null wash, namespace collision, `kitRef` with
`talentMods`, a flat plugin potential), schema parity via `tools/records-schema.mjs`, the engine clamps, served i18n bytes inside a content `cs`,
route-only ground paths, actual kit/effect behavior, mode overrides, immutable bytes and hashes, unchanged P0 identity,
and patched bonds through Node and the actual `loadBrowserSim` loader with its data shim. The 287 vanilla golden
scenarios are unchanged. Normative references updated: design/engine.md kit dispatch, design/match.md mode overrides,
SIM.md §7.2/§7.3, DATA.md generation and integrity contracts, PLUGINS.md §6 and §10.

### 32.6 P1c — room content sets

Seen: the design study's §6 (one content set per room, a match host per set: its 方案 B), §7 (the panel, the unofficial
marks), §10 「P1c」, §12 (risks) and its appendix A (the protocol increments); the 0.3.0 plan's C1 (WP-A's content set),
C3 (the room protocol) and C4 (the battle runner stays as it is: this step only adds the `cs` check). The reference is
PLUGINS.md §11; the protocol increments are `docs/design/network.md` §8.

**The room's plugins** (`server/plugins/roomSets.js`). `room.setPlugins` now takes content plugins beside the cosmetic
ones. One list is closed over its requirements and ordered (`shared/plugins/order.js`), then split by class: the cosmetic
plugins, and a content plugin's own `assets`, form the visual set (`vs`, §32.3); the content plugins form the content set
(its own `cs`). Refusals: PLUGIN_UNKNOWN, PLUGIN_BLOCKED (a class or scope rooms cannot take; the server owner's policy;
a set whose merged data does not validate — the builder's first problems as the detail), PLUGIN_DEPENDENCY /
PLUGIN_CONFLICT, PLUGIN_INCOMPATIBLE (a gameplay plugin made for other game versions or another plugin API), PLUGIN_BUSY
(one content set more than `limits.maxSets`), NOT_HOST, ROOM_STARTED. A change of the content set or of the mode
un-readies the other players like a difficulty change. `room.state.content` becomes `{ cs, vanilla: false, plugins,
warnings, bytes, dataBytes, vs?, modes?, modeId?, kittedExtra? }` and each human seat and spectator carries `content:
'loading' | 'ready' | 'failed'` — only in such a room; a vanilla room's state is byte for byte what it was.

**The content sets** (`server/plugins/contentSets.js`) adapt WP-A's C1 (`buildContentSet` / `contentSetFiles`, §32.5)
to the room: kept by `cs`, held while a room uses one, the last 8 nobody holds kept for their `/cs/` files; `hashInput`
(what a page and a match host recompute `cs` from, out of the set's index: the data hashes, the translation hashes since
WP-A's review round, the plugins and their settings) is checked against the builder's `cs` before a set is used;
`modes` are the `config.modes` records the vanilla data lacks, as `{ id, name, roomMode, difficulty }`; `kittedExtra`
the operators the set adds to a 自选 slot's list. The same plugins in the same state (each one's manifest and content
hash) with the same settings — normalized as the builder reads them (`normalizeSettings`: a key a plugin does not
declare is dropped, so it never costs a build) — are not built again: a build takes 250–430 ms of the server's thread
(measured with a plugin of one patch: 434 ms cold, 248 ms for the same set inside the builder). `limits.maxSets` is
checked before anything is built (a set no room holds while the limit is reached is refused unbuilt), and a set is
remembered only once it is kept (the review's HIGH-2: junk settings rebuilt the set on every request, after the limit
too). Until WP-A's branch was merged a stub
stood in (P1c's first commits); it is gone.

**The match hosts** (`server/match/host/`). A vanilla room's match — and a room with cosmetic plugins only, whose `cs` is
the vanilla one — runs in the server process exactly as before: the lobby still does `new MatchClass(opts)`. A content
set's matches run in one match host per `cs`, shared by the rooms that play that set: a child process
(`child_process.fork`; the default since the review, below) or, asked for with `SP_MATCH_HOST=worker`, a worker thread —
"worker" below means either. `entry.js` receives the set's
data bytes (exactly the bytes served) and its hash input, checks every file's SHA-256 and the composition against `cs`,
installs the data (`server/data.js` `setData`, new) and only then imports `Match.js`: the worker's module table — the
sim's DataSource (`nodeData.js` reads `server/data.js`), the kit and meta registries, `content/support`'s caches — is
that set's alone, built fresh in a new isolate, so nothing needs resetting. `proxy.js` is the lobby's `room.match` for
such a match: the same MATCH INTERFACE, its answers Promises, its requests answered in order. `index.js` starts a worker
on a set's first match, keeps it while it has matches and 10 minutes more, runs at most `maxWorkers` (4: a new set stops
an idle worker first, else PLUGIN_BUSY), limits each one (`resourceLimits`: 768 MB old generation, 64 MB young, 4 MB
stack) and watches it: a heartbeat every second, terminated after 10 s of silence. A worker that hangs, crashes, runs
out of memory or does not boot ends its matches with `summary.reason` 'plugin-hang' / 'plugin-crash' / 'plugin-start';
each room goes back to its lobby and its members read why (an m.toast); the log names the set and its plugins.

Measured on Node v22.22.0 (macOS; a worker thread with `maxOldGenerationSizeMb: 160`, a child process with
`--max-old-space-size=160`): in a worker thread many small objects end the worker alone (ERR_WORKER_OUT_OF_MEMORY, the
main process alive), but one large allocation can abort the **whole process** (exit 134): `new Array(4e7).fill(1)`
("CALL_AND_RETRY_LAST Allocation failed", the review's repro), and in my runs an array grown by `push` ("Reached heap
limit") and a `Map` grown by `set` — the review saw those two end at ERR_WORKER_OUT_OF_MEMORY instead, so which one
happens depends on the allocation. A child process dies alone (SIGABRT) in every case. The review found a data plugin
that reaches the `fill`: `factions.generation.minReplacedEnemyCount` 4e7 → `replacedCount` → every replaced action's
unit count → the bounty plan's `new Array(own).fill(…)` — an ordinary four-player match with a bounty. Since then
(the integration decision of 2026-10-14) a child process per set is the default and a worker thread is an opt-in
(`SP_MATCH_HOST=worker`, the `transport: 'worker'` option); and `server/match/waves.js` clamps `replacedCount`'s min / max
and the bounty plan's own units to a template action's 50 (templateSpawns' clamp; the official 1 / 5 are unchanged).
The validator bounds the `factions.generation` numbers a plugin may set since the integration (§32.5: the 10× rule, the
replaced counts within the same 50, `PLUGIN_ACTION_UNITS`). A child process starts
in about 0.2 s and holds about 117 MB (RSS) once the data and the match code are loaded (measured).

**The asynchronous lobby.** `server/net.js` answers a handler's Promise when it settles (the same `ok` / `error`
reply, the same `rid`). A room with a hosted match handles its messages and events one at a time on a queue of its own
(`Lobby.serial`): a request — `room.*` and the match's own messages alike — waits for the previous one's answer, and a
disconnect, an expiry and a resync take their turn on the same queue. A vanilla room never queues: its match answers at
once, as before. A `room.contentReady` counts only for the page that is there: one from a session whose socket is gone is answered
OK and writes nothing, and one that waited on the queue across a disconnect of its seat (each disconnect bumps the
seat's generation, captured when the message was queued) is dropped the same way — the review's MED-3: a reloaded page
was shown as checked, and could ready, before it had checked a byte. The proxy answers
a request the worker has not answered within 15 s with INTERNAL (the watchdog acts first on a living worker), and a
request still pending when its match ends is answered by the worker, not refused as WRONG_PHASE.

**The page** (`public/js/content.js`, `data.js`, `battle/runner.js`). When `room.state.content` names a gameplay set the
page fetches `/cs/<cs16>/index.json` and every file it lists as bytes — an unchanged one at its plain `/data/…?v=` URL
(cached for good), a patched one at `/cs/<cs16>/…`, four at a time, two retries — and checks each body's size and
SHA-256 **before decoding** (a BOM is part of the bytes), the composition against the room's `cs`, and that the manifest
names no address but `/data/` and `/cs/<cs16>/`. `data.js` then shows the set (`setContent`: every screen reads its
operators, modes and texts; the server's files come back without a download when the room leaves it), the runner
simulates on it, and the page sends `room.contentReady { cs }` — again when its seat shows it not ready (a reconnect) —
or `{ cs, ok: false }` with one toast. `room.ready` waits for the player's own page, `room.start` for every player's
(CONTENT_NOT_READY). The runner's only change (C4): a battle whose spec names a `cs` the page does not hold is watched
as a replica, never run as the authority — no `b.progress`, no `b.result` — and the server takes the field over at the
deadline; the authority's `b.result` carries its `cs`, and a match with gameplay plugins (`opts.requireCs`) refuses a
result naming another one like an implausible result (the server simulates the battle). A BattleSpec may carry `vs`
(diagnostics; the sim never reads it).

**The interface.** The 插件 panel lists content plugins with 改变玩法 and 非官方 badges, the host's switches (the others
read them), the room's mode (`room.setMode`: an official mode of the room's type sets the difficulty, a plugin mode
becomes `content.modeId`), the page's download with its progress, ready or failed; a gameplay plugin made for other
versions shows why it cannot be switched on. The room's tag reads 非官方, each seat its 插件内容 state, and the start
button waits for them. The briefing, the result and the HUD corner show the unofficial mark (「非官方」, MOD on the HUD;
its tooltip counts the gameplay plugins). Statistics records name the set (`content: { cs, plugins }`, stats v2 — a v1
record migrates as the original game); the overview leaves such matches out by default and 「含插件对局」 counts them —
a match with cosmetic plugins only counts as the original game (the owner's decision of 2026-10-13). The diagnostics
line reads `MODDED cs=… vs=… mode=… plugins=…`; the bug template asks whether plugins were on. The loadout sync resends
when the room's content changes, the server filters a stored loadout, 干员持有 list or 自选 roster of another content
to the room's data (an unknown id dropped, the rest kept — the design study's §6.9), and the 自选 picker offers the
set's added operators.

**The server owner's policy** (`server/plugins/policy.js`): an optional `plugins/config.json` — `rooms` (host / fixed /
off; `SP_PLUGINS` overrides it), `default`, `allow`, `classes`, `limits.maxSets`, `allowIncompatible` — read again when
it changes; a bad field keeps its default and is logged. DEPLOY.md documents it.

**Merged with the other work packages.** WP-A's `claude/mod-p1b` (fc768aa8, then its review fixes 5a1e0e95 and the
official bounds 36d65d1c) replaced the stub; the review round's translation hashes in `cs` reached the page's and the hosts' recomputation, and its
potential rule for plugin operators the lobby's filter (a potential that changes nothing is dropped, its 练度 kept).
`claude/030-int` (d4891332: P2's DSL, P4's interface plugins) is merged too: `dsl/compile.js` is P2's compiler, and
WP-A2 accepts `dsl` in data plugins (battle specs on `kit.dsl`, prep specs in the `dsl` part). The registry reads a content
plugin's art and a ui plugin's frame side by side, and the panel shows both the room's plugins and P4's recommendations
(P4 sends no protocol message; `uiRecommend` stays its own).

[ASSUMED] choices beyond the design study:

- 4 concurrent content sets (`limits.maxSets`) and 4 match hosts; 8 sets kept for their `/cs/` files when no room holds
  them; a host idle 10 minutes stops; heartbeat 1 s, watchdog 10 s; 768 MB old generation per host (a worker thread also
  64 MB young, 4 MB stack); a proxied request 15 s. (The default transport is no assumption any more: a child process,
  decided after the review.)
- PLUGIN_BUSY for the concurrent-set limit (the study's draft named PLUGIN_LIMIT, which P1a uses for a visual set's art
  limits); PLUGIN_INCOMPATIBLE, PLUGIN_BUSY and CONTENT_NOT_READY join `shared/constants.js` ERR and must be merged into
  the error catalogue (C6).
- The policy file's name and fields; `allow: ["*"]` by default; rooms fixed means exactly the `default` set.
- A cosmetic plugin's requirements are cosmetic plugins; a content plugin may require a cosmetic one and may carry art.
- The page fetches four files at once, 60 s per file, retries after 0.6 s and 2 s; a failed check is told once.
- The plugin hash in `room.state.content.plugins[].hash` is the first 16 hex digits of the set's plugin hash (as a
  cosmetic plugin's).
- `kittedExtra` (only the added operators, up to 200) rather than the whole 自选 list in every `room.state`.
- Spectators load and check the set too and show their state, but never gate the start.

Tests (targeted, `node --test …`): `test/lobby-plugins.test.js` (20), `test/match/plugin-host.test.js` (5: a whole
match of AI seats in a worker on the set's data and mode; `SP_VERIFY=all` in a worker — a result naming the room's `cs`
verified and accepted, one naming none refused and run by the server; the watchdog on a worker that stops beating, and
a worker that throws;
a child process that runs out of memory — the fixture allocates without end, since the data that once did it is
refused now; the worker limit; the official-bounds snapshot the host installs), `test/ui/plugins-content.test.js` (15),
`test/plugins/contentSets.test.js` (2), and the browser test `test/ui/plugins-content.e2e.test.js` (`SP_E2E=1`: two
pages, a data plugin, both check the set, a battle on it, the host's result accepted, a page claiming another `cs` no
authority). The 287 golden scenarios are unchanged.

Open points: a settings form in the panel (the protocol takes settings); a notice when a content plugin changes on disk
while a room holds its set (cosmetic plugins have one, §32.3.1); `allowIncompatible` cannot take effect while the
builder refuses a plugin it marks incompatible (WP-A); a room's queue has no length limit
beyond the connection's rate limits. (Settled at integration: the `factions.generation` bounds, §32.5; the official-bounds
table — the match host and the page install the content set's snapshot, so a modded battle's caps are the validator's
10 × official maximum on both sides, not the floor.)

### 32.7 P2 DSL-lite

A declarative kit. `compileDsl(spec, { bb, chess, def })` in `server/sim/content/dsl/compile.js` returns a Kit, the same
object a kit function returns. The author reference is [PLUGIN-DSL.md](../PLUGIN-DSL.md). `schemas/dsl.schema.json` is
the closed vocabulary (C7). Prep effects compile with `server/match/dslMeta.js` (`compileDslMeta` / `registerDslMeta`)
onto a MetaRegistry the caller owns. Nothing in the sim imports the prep module, and nothing on the vanilla boot path
imports the compiler. A content plugin puts a battle spec on the compact operator as `kit.dsl` and prep specs in the
manifest `dsl` part. The builder validates a battle spec with `validateDsl` and a prep spec with `validatePrepSpec`
(`server/sim/content/dsl/validate.js`). On success the battle spec is the chess record's `dsl` (`def.raw.dsl`) and
`kitOf` calls `compileDsl`. Prep specs are kept in a builder-private map and written to `data.dsl[<plugin id>]` only
after every ordinary patch, and therefore enter `cs`. A vanilla set has no `data.dsl`. The match host
(`server/match/host/entry.js`) calls `registerDslMeta` on that process's MetaRegistry after `setData` and before
importing Match. An invalid spec does not throw from the validator; `compileDsl` throws an Error whose `.code` is
the first problem. Those `PLUGIN_DSL_*` codes are in the error catalogue (`shared/plugins/errors.js`). A direct
literal past the official cap uses the existing `PLUGIN_DATA_BOUND`.

The battle vocabulary is the design study's list: triggers `always / deploy / battleStart / attack / hitDealt /
hitTaken / kill / allyDeath / skillStart / skillEnd / every / hpBelow`, conditions `chance / targetHas / selfHpBelow /
enemiesInRange / bondActive / skillActive`, actions `buff / status / damage / heal / sp / dp / push / pull /
spawnToken / layers / shield / fx`, values as numbers or `{bb}` / `{talent:[i,key]}` / `{mul|add|min|max}`, targets
`self / target / source / alliesInRange / enemiesInRange / enemiesInRadius / lowestHpAlly / allAllies`. Only
addition, subtraction, multiplication and division. No loops and no state outside the battle. Damage this compiler
deals carries a `dsl` tag, and a DSL `hitDealt` / `hitTaken` handler ignores that tag (SIM.md §5).

Prep triggers `roundStart / income / buy / sell / refresh / merge / levelUp / battleResult` map to `onRoundStart /
onIncome / onBuy / onSold / onRefresh / onMerge / onLevelUp / onBattleResult`. Actions `funds / layers / gainItem` call
`addFunds` / `addLayers` / `grantItem`.

[ASSUMED], each marked in the code as well:

- The action budget defaults to 8000 executions per plugin per battle. `spec.budget` may set a positive integer up to
  1e7 (the validator rejects anything else; the counter itself trusts a limit the compiler already stored). One action
  node costs one, however many targets it fans out to. `then` nodes cost their own. An empty target list still costs
  one. A failed structural condition does not. `chance` is drawn only after the other checks pass, and a failed chance
  does not spend budget. Past the limit that plugin stops for the rest of the battle: its timers are cancelled, buffs
  tagged `plugin:<id>` are removed, skill mods the engine already applied stay until the skill ends, and one
  `plugin:<id>` error goes through `battle._handlerError`. The overflowing action does not run and does not increment
  the counter. Other plugins are left alone. State is a WeakMap keyed by the battle.
- `{sub}` and `{div}` are the minus and divide forms. A non-finite number becomes 0. Division by 0 is `PLUGIN_DSL_DIV0`
  in the validator when the divisor is a literal 0, and 0 at runtime otherwise, never Infinity. An empty `min` / `max`
  is 0.
- `{trait}` reads the trait blackboard. `{stat, of}` reads `atk / maxHp / hp / def / res` of `self` (the default),
  `target`, `source` or `lastSpawn` when the action runs. `{batMod}` applies the kit helper `batMod` with the selected
  skill's description, because a raw `base_attack_time` is a flat change of seconds.
- `{talent:[i,key]}` is the i-th named talent (talents whose index is −1 are skipped), not a raw array index.
- `then` may sit beside the action or inside that action's body (the Texas rewrite puts it inside `damage`). Writing
  both is `PLUGIN_DSL_ACTION`. For damage, status, buff, heal, sp, push, pull and shield it runs once per target,
  after that target and before the next. Other actions run it once afterwards. Nesting stops at 4. `times` on damage
  repeats that one node from 1 to 8 and stops early if the target dies.
- `enemiesInRadius` defaults to `foesInRadius` from the operator (a skill may not select an untargetable or
  unrevealed stealth enemy), written `{ "enemiesInRadius": <r> }`. `at` (`self` / `target` / `source` / `lastSpawn`)
  moves the centre. A missing `lastSpawn` selects nobody and does not fall back to the operator. `shape: "chebyshev"`
