  or `includeStealth: true` scans `battle.enemies` in that order, skips dead, hidden and untargetable units, and skips
  stealth unless `includeStealth` is set. Chebyshev distance uses `cheb` and includes a distance of `r`. 风丸's origami
  is chebyshev ≤ 1 around the summon, stealth included; the fx radius 1.5 is the visual, not the hit. `lowestHpAlly`
  is the injured ally `lowestHpAllyInRange` returns. `allAllies` is `alliesFor(unit, unit.ownerId)`: the same owner
  only, including on a unite field. `enemiesInRange` / `alliesInRange` use the unit's current range, so a skill that
  must strike with its skill grid sets `targeting.rangeGrid` (and `noRangeExtend` when the official grid is applied
  with extend 0).
- `hitDealt` is the `hit` hook for this unit. `hitTaken` is `damaged` on this unit, and only `byEnemyAttack` (流失,
  element, a sourceless hit, a counter and a reflect do not count). `kill` fires for this unit as killer. `allyDeath`
  is another ally of the same owner. `attack` uses the first target. `deploy` / `skillStart` / `skillEnd` require this
  unit. `always` runs once. A unit is not alive while the battle is constructed, so `always` waits for that unit's
  first `deploy` and does not run again on a later redeploy. An install on a unit that is already alive still runs
  immediately. `hpBelow` fires on the edge when `hpRatio` crosses below `below`, and stays
  latched until the ratio recovers. `every` uses `battle.every` (first due one interval later; the validator rejects a
  period shorter than one tick, 1/30 s). Cooldown is seconds kept on that install, and it resets when `deploySeq`
  changes, so two copies of one chess do not share it. A dead unit skips the action except on `battleStart`,
  `allyDeath` and `deploy`. `always` does not run while the unit is still down.
- `damage.lose: true` calls `loseHp`. `minRemain` skips the loss when it would leave HP below that remainder.
  `damage.from: "lastSpawn"` uses the token just spawned as the source. `dp` with `fx: true` emits `fx('dp')` even when
  the amount is 0. `spawnToken` takes `tile: "around"` (a free standable tile) or an absolute `{r,c}`. An absolute tile
  must be inside the rect, not reserved, and `canStand` (a start or end glyph is refused). Failure matches `around`:
  only `stop: true` halts the rest of the list. The coordinate is not limited to the owner's half: a standable road on
  the other half of a unite field is still accepted. A token id is a string, or `{ override, match, fallback }` where
  `match` is a list of substrings tried after `chess.skill.overrideTokenKey`. There is no relative `{dr,dc}` tile.
  `fx: true` on the spawn emits `fx('summon')`.
- A buff's duration, when omitted, is the engine's own default. A shield is visible unless `visible` is false.
- `funds.setIncome: true` assigns `ev.income` (truncated, at least 0) and does not call `addFunds`. A round that fails
  `if.round` does not touch a value already on the event. Prep has no per-plugin action budget. Prep conditions are
  `round`, `chance` and `bondActive`. `bondActive` reads the prep ctx (`ctx.bond(id)`, else `ctx.bonds[id]`; `active`
  from that record, or `ctx.bondActive(id)` when the record has no `active` field; `tier` from the record). A condition
  that needs a unit (`selfHpBelow`, `enemiesInRange`, `skillActive`, `targetHas`) is `PLUGIN_DSL_CONDITION` at validate
  time. The Chinese text includes 「备战条件不能用这一条」. It is not a silent false.
- The host plugin id is required. `opts.pluginId` and `opts.def.raw.pluginId` are the trusted sources. If both are set
  and they differ, or the author's `spec.pluginId` differs from the host, or no host id was given, compilation throws
  `PLUGIN_DSL_PLUGIN_ID`. There is no shared `"dsl"` budget bucket. A value object is exactly one operation
  (`PLUGIN_DSL_VALUE`, 「取值对象恰好一个运算」). Each expression is capped at 64 nodes and 64 levels
  (`PLUGIN_DSL_VALUE` / `PLUGIN_DSL_LOOP`) so a deep `{add}` chain does not throw `RangeError`.
- A plugin meta key is only `effect:<this host plugin id>:<local>`. `registerDslMeta` refuses `band:` / `global:` and
  another plugin's `effect:` key and does not call `register`. The schema checks the `effect:<id>:<local>` shape only;
  it does not compare the id segment to the host. `validateDsl` uses the same shape, so the two agree. Registration is
  what enforces the host prefix.
- [ASSUMED] The schema is draft-07 so the Ajv 6 already present in the install can compile it. It does not enforce the
  64-node cap, the 64-level cap, or `then` depth 4. A workbench that trusts only the schema can accept those. `compile`
  / `validateDsl` reject them.
- [ASSUMED] Omitting `spec.pluginId` is allowed. A written one must equal the host. The builder stamps `record.pluginId`
  separately and does not rewrite the author's spec. `validateData` checks the merged record again: `rec.pluginId`
  must be the plugin that contributed it when an owner map is passed. Patching `dsl` onto an official chess (no owner)
  is `PLUGIN_DSL_PLUGIN_ID`.
- [ASSUMED] A manifest `dsl` file is prep-only and must include `meta`. A battle kit lives on the operator. The meta
  key must be `effect:<this plugin id>:<local>` with a non-empty local part, and `meta.on` must be a non-empty array.
  `validatePrepSpec` enforces that while the content set is built. `registerDslMeta` repeats the host-prefix check,
  including `global:` and another plugin's key. `contentData` scan and `node tools/plugin.mjs check` stay shape-only
  for that prefix and for numeric caps: a foreign key still loads the plugin, and `check --strict` can pass it.
  `buildContentSet` / `contents.resolve` is the admission gate (no `cs`; the resolve detail carries `PLUGIN_DSL_META`,
  and the error code is `PLUGIN_BLOCKED` because `PLUGIN_DSL_META` is not a room `ERR`). A set that skips the builder
  but still hashes fails in the match host: `boot()` throws, the child posts `boot-error`, and the room ends
  `plugin-start`. That is a different layer from a battle content fault.
- [ASSUMED] A content-set DSL meta spec is on for every player of that set. After registration the host wraps Match
  and, once players exist, pushes `{ id: 'dsl:'+key, key, battle: false, hidden: true }` onto each `ps.effects`.
  Authors cannot register `global:`. The grant is what makes the handler run: registration alone does not.
- [ASSUMED] `data.dsl` is a reserved table. Data patches with `file: "dsl"` are `PLUGIN_DSL_PLUGIN_ID` ("data patches
  cannot write the reserved dsl table") and are not passed to `applyPatches`. The table is attached only from the
  builder's private accumulator, and only when that accumulator is non-empty and the set has no problems. An empty
  accumulator does not invent `data.dsl` on a vanilla set.
- [ASSUMED] Only the record owner may write its DSL, even when a later plugin declares `requires` and `dsl`. The
  owner key is `chess.<id>` or `units.<id>` (a backup form belongs to the unit, not to the form key). The same plugin
  may replace its own DSL. An official record has no owner, so a DSL patch on it is `PLUGIN_DSL_PLUGIN_ID`. A write
  without the `dsl` permission is also `PLUGIN_CONTENT_PERMISSION`. Both codes are reported when both fail.
- [ASSUMED] Potential and loadout overlays are stat leaves. `potDown` (and any path whose segments are `dsl` or
  `pluginId`) cannot carry behavior or identity. The builder refuses a new or changed leaf with `PLUGIN_DSL_PLUGIN_ID`.
  `applyPotDown` skips those paths and does not create the leaf. A stat leaf such as `stats.atk` is unchanged.
  Form-level `dsl` goes through the same permission and owner scan. Composed potential variants are not each
  re-validated.
- [ASSUMED] DSL numbers use the WP-A official-bounds snapshot (`shared/plugins/officialBounds.js`,
  `max(10 × official max, 64)`), read at call time. The correspondence in `server/sim/content/dsl/bounds.js` is:
  `mods.atkPct` / `atkFlat` → blackboard `atk` (cap 300); `defPct` / `defFlat` → `def`; `maxHpPct` / `maxHpFlat` /
  `hpFlat` → `max_hp`; `resPct` / `resFlat` → `magic_resistance`; `aspd` / `attackSpeed` → `attack_speed` (not the
  absent key `aspd`, whose floor would be 64); any other mod key, including `taunt` and `batPct`, → `atk`, so a typo
  cannot carry `1e9`. `skill.ammo` → blackboard `ammo` (cap 64; official max 3). `skill.spCost` → field
  `skill.spCost` (cap 1200). `skill.initSp` → field `skill.initSp` (cap 1100), not `skill.spCost`. `skill.charges` /
  `maxCharges` → those fields; vanilla skills do not carry them, so the floor 64 applies and a count above 64 is
  refused. `attack.atkScale` → blackboard `atk_scale` (cap 80). `attack.splashRadius` → blackboard `radius` (cap 64).
  `attack.maxTargets` and `targeting.maxTargets` → blackboard `max_target` (cap 100). Direct literals are rejected
  with `PLUGIN_DATA_BOUND` only when the caller passes `{ bounds: true }` and `rememberOfficialBounds` has installed
  the snapshot (`composeContentSet` does that before expand). `validateDsl` without that option, or with an empty
  cache, does not apply the floor, so a scan stays shape-only even after another build warmed the cache. Nested
  factors inside `{mul}` / `{add}` are not admission-rejected; the expression result is clamped to the same cap in
  `compile.js` / `resolveMods`. A product of in-range literals can still exceed the cap and is clamped (`{mul:[100,100]}`
  on `atkPct` becomes 300, and ATK 200 becomes 60200). When the snapshot is missing, the runtime clamp uses the floor.
  `damage.times` stays 1–8 and `every.seconds` stays ≥ 1/30. These paths are not capped, and that is the residual:
  `duration`, action amounts (damage / heal / sp / dp / shield / layers), `rangeExtend`, and buff / status durations.
  The 64-node and 64-level caps remain validate-only; the schema does not count them.
- [ASSUMED] If `compileDsl` still throws after a set was built, the content set is corrupt. `setupUnitKit` does not
  return `{ skill: null, talents: [] }` and does not substitute the generic kit. A throw whose code matches
  `PLUGIN_DSL_*`, or any throw while `raw.dsl` is set (including `PLUGIN_DATA_BOUND`), sets `battle.contentFault`
  (`layer: 'setupUnitKit'`) and rethrows. `installContent` logs it once as `content:dsl` and rethrows.
  `_setupUnit` rethrows before `u.kit = {}`. The `Battle` constructor does not call `start()` or `forceEnd` (a
  non-timeout `forceEnd` would fold the reason and call `start()`, which deploys). It sets `finished`, `started` and
  `reason: 'forced'`, and stores `_buildResult()` (or the same fallback object `_hardFinish` uses). `result().errors`
  is greater than 0. `battle.errors` carries the label `content:dsl` and the code. The operator does not fight.
  Host and page share this constructor. The player sees the ordinary 战斗结束 box; there is no dedicated DSL string.
  This is not the room `plugin-start` path. A throw that is neither a `PLUGIN_DSL_*` code nor a DSL record still
  falls back to the generic kit. `forceEnd`'s reason folding is left as it was.
- [ASSUMED] The budget WeakMap is not in a snapshot. Resuming mid-battle from a snapshot would count from zero. This
  branch does not resume from snapshots. Cutoff removes buffs tagged `plugin:<id>` and cancels that plugin's timers.
  Statuses from `applyStatus` and summons already on the field are not tagged and are not recalled. A unite or boss
  battle is one shared counter for the whole battle, not one per seat.
- [ASSUMED] 风丸's `onEnd` retreat is still not a DSL verb. The compare stops before the skill duration (20 s). If the
  battle runs to the end of the skill, the official token is gone and the DSL token is still alive. A far enemy that
  neither circle hits is not evidence that the splash matches. The splash compare is the neighbour on the far side of
  the summon plus one stealthed enemy. Compact `dmg` events do not carry tags; the damage info still has `dsl` on top
  of the author's tags, so a tag-sensitive consumer does not see a bit-exact official hit.
- sim-check was not run for this package.

Five official effects are reproduced by fixtures, not by editing the official kits: 地灵's duration attack buff,
隐现's ammo skill (the talent that waits 20 s is left out of the short window), 德克萨斯's sword rain plus the
battle-start DP talent, 风丸's summon inside the window above (the origami centre is the summon, chebyshev ≤ 1,
stealth included; the retreat on `onEnd` is not encoded), and 老鲤's round income, which is a prep dispatch rather
than a battle and is not registered on `band:band_lmlee`. Golden scenes are unchanged because the vanilla path never
reads this compiler.

### 32.8 P4 — interface plugins

An interface plugin is a page the server serves and the player's own browser shows. It does not join a room's
`plugins[]`, it sends no protocol message, and it cannot play the match. The word in the interface is 「插件」.

**What loads.** Class `ui` is `supported`, so the registry lists it `loaded` (`shared/plugins/manifest.js`). `script`
and `trusted` stay unsupported. A ui manifest names `entry.client` (an `.html` file; permission `ui.frame`) and
`slots` — `hud.right`, `result.tab`, `stats.tab`, `room.panel` (`UI_SLOT_IDS`). An absent or empty `slots` is a
warning and the plugin still loads; the host grants it no slot. A bad list, a duplicate, more than those four, or
`slots` on another class, is a problem. `description` is the 简介. The frame files (`.html`, `.js`, `.css`, `.png`)
must sit under the directory of `entry.client`. A folder whose name starts with `_` is never scanned;
`plugins/_template/ui/` is the example (a damage tally) and is copied by a server owner under a real id.

**Serving and `vs`.** The registry (`readUiFrame` in `server/plugins/registry.js`) hashes the frame files the way a
cosmetic overlay is hashed and names the plugin with `visualHashOf` (`shared/plugins/visual.js` — not a second hash).
`writes` is the canonical `{ entry, slots }`; `plugin.json` is in the composition, so editing the manifest changes
`vs`, and is not served. The catalogue entry of a loaded ui plugin adds `slots`, `entry` (a path inside the plugin),
`vs`, `frameBytes` and `frameFiles` (`{ path: sha256 }`). It does not add `artBytes` and it is not a visual set: a
room's `resolve` still accepts only cosmetic ids (`PLUGIN_BLOCKED` for a ui id). The files are served at
`/p/<id>/<hash8>/<path>` with the same traversal, symlink, identity and hash8 rules as cosmetic art. Responses of a
ui plugin's files, and only those, carry `Content-Security-Policy: ` the string `UI_FRAME_CSP`, on 200, 206 and
304 (one header object). There is no site-wide CSP (that stays P3b). A 416 is an empty body and does not carry it.

**`cs`.** A ui plugin is not an input of `cs`. Installing one, or editing its page, does not change `cs` of a build.
`cs` does hash every served `/shared/**.js`, so this phase's new `shared/plugins/ui.js` (and any edit of
`shared/plugins/identity.js`) changes the vanilla `cs` relative to the parent commit. Relative to the same build with
`plugins: []` and no ui folder, the hash is unchanged. Golden results are untouched.

**The frame.** The host sets an iframe
`sandbox="allow-scripts"` (no `allow-same-origin`: the origin is the string `"null"`), `referrerpolicy="no-referrer"`,
`allow=""`. [ASSUMED] `credentialless` is set with `setAttribute` where the browser has it. The iframe's `src` is the
plugin's own `/p/<id>/<hash8>/<entry>` (`framePageUrl`, checked by `frameEntryAllowed`). The host counts loads
(`frameLoadDecision`): the first is the entry, and any later one destroys the iframe, clears `ready` and posts nothing
further. Moving the iframe between the park and a slot makes the browser reload it from its `src` (the entry), so a
move the host makes itself (`moveFrame`) resets the count and `ready` first; a navigation the plugin starts is never
such a move. (The review round first used a redirect page, `plugin-frame.html`, that navigated from its own load handler;
Chrome gives no iframe `load` event for such a page, so the entry looked like the redirect, never got `welcome` and
never took its slot — the browser test caught it. The redirect is gone; the response header and the iframe attribute
already sandbox the page.) A plugin message is
applied on the next turn, so a `load` that was already queued runs first. `ready` is accepted only while the count is
still the entry. `targetOrigin '*'` is required because the origin is the string `"null"`, and `"null"` does not tell
the entry document from a later opaque document — changing it is not a substitute for stopping the feed. The host
accepts a message only from that iframe's window and only when `event.origin === 'null'`. Slot elements use an empty
`dangerouslySetInnerHTML` so a Preact re-render does not remove the foreign iframe. A frame that a re-render detaches
is parked and put back; a frame the host itself moved stays where it was moved.

`UI_FRAME_CSP` includes `sandbox allow-scripts` (no `allow-same-origin`), `frame-ancestors 'self'`, `base-uri 'none'`
and `form-action 'none'`. A `<meta>` sandbox is ignored, so the response header is what sandboxes the document when
the same URL is opened at the top level (`/p/<id>/<hash8>/<entry>.html`, or the redirect above). The hash is not a
gate: any 8 hex digits still answer 200 with `no-cache` and this header. Do not add `allow-same-origin` to make
`'self'` match the opaque origin.

[ASSUMED] Data the host has already posted can be written into the URL of the navigation that leaves the entry.
Stopping later posts does not get that copy back. Delivering less is the only control that works; a navigation CSP
directive (`navigate-to`) was not measured in a browser and is not this fix. The host cannot read the frame's URL.
A plugin that leaves the entry before that document fires `load` can make the next completed load look like the
entry, and one parent task already queued before the third `load` can still post once.

**postMessage v1** (`shared/plugins/ui.js`, schema `schemas/ui.schema.json`). Host → plugin: `welcome` `{ id, slots }`
(the slots the manifest declared), `state` `{ public, private }`, `battle` `{ fieldId, gt, events, snap, truncated }`,
`stats` `{ unitId, fieldId, stats }`, `storage`, `limit`, `slot`, `rejected`. Plugin → host: `ready`, `slot`,
`resize`, `storage` (`get` / `set` / `delete` / `keys`), `notice`, `stats` (a read of the battle on screen — the host
answers from `battleRunner.unitStats` and never sends `g.unitStats`). Every message is shape-checked and rate-limited.
A message whose type is a client→server intent (`hello`, `ping`, `room.*`, `g.*`, `b.*`), or that carries one, is
`rejected` with reason `intent`. Nothing in this bridge calls `net.send`.

**Subsets [ASSUMED].** `subsetPublic`: `phase`, `round`, `lastRound`, `modeId`, `difficulty`, `paused`, and up to 8
players of `playerId`, `seat`, `name`, `alive`, `lp`, `shopLevel`. `subsetPrivate`: `playerId`, `seat`, `alive`, `lp`,
`funds`, `bandId`, `ready`; `shop` of `level` and up to 16 slots of `kind`, `id`, `price`, `sold`; `hand` / `board` as
`uid`, `kind`, `id`, `chessId`, `level`; `stats` of `dmgDealt`, `kills`, `leaks`, `gold`; up to 32 bonds of `bondId`,
`count`, `tier`. Dropped on purpose: loadout, ops, diy, effects, nextEnemies, personalChoice, rewardOffer, tokens,
hiddenBossId, anyone else's hand.

**Battle events.** Sim tuples are mapped by position, not by "the first number". `damage.js` writes
`['dmg', target.id, amount, kind]` (a crit appends `'r'`) and `['heal', target.id, amount]`: index 1 is the unit id,
index 2 is the HP. `atk` is source id, target id, kind, and has no amount. A tuple whose payload is an object
(`spawn`'s unitInfo, which can carry loadout and diy) keeps the tag only. Object messages still keep the allowlisted
keys, so `{ t: 'damage', amount: 4 }` stays an amount. The example tally adds `amount` when `t` is `dmg` (and the
object form `damage`). It does not look at `hit` or `hpDamage`.

**Limits [ASSUMED],** the same list as the header of `shared/plugins/ui.js`: 20 messages a second per plugin, burst
40; battle pushes at 4 Hz, at most 40 events a batch; a snapshot whose JSON text is longer than 48 KB (JavaScript
string length) is omitted and `truncated` is true; `plainClone` also sets `truncated` when it cuts a string to 120
characters, an array to 32 items, an object to 24 keys, or a value past depth 4, and the partial snapshot is still
sent when it fits — `truncated: false` means those caps did not fire; a plugin→host message over 8 KB refused;
storage keys `^[A-Za-z0-9._-]{1,64}$`, not `__proto__` / `prototype` / `constructor` / `..`, a JSON value ≤ 4096
UTF-8 bytes (`TextEncoder` on the JSON text, not JavaScript string length), ≤ 32 keys and ≤ 32 KB UTF-8 per plugin,
namespaced `sp.plugin.<id>.<key>` in the host page's `localStorage` (the iframe has none of its own); height clamped
to 48–480 px; a notice ≤ 80 characters; a distinct notice at most once per 2 seconds (the same text is still
delivered, so the toast host can merge it); one slot per plugin, one plugin per slot, at most 8 plugins mounted.
The host strips `t: 'b.snap'` before a snapshot is given to the plugin (the message's own type is `battle`).
`isMatchIntent` does not treat that host label as a client intent; a nested `g.*` / `room.*` / other `b.*` still is.
[ASSUMED] the host grants the first declared slot that has an element on the page and is free, otherwise the first
free declared slot.
[ASSUMED] `UI_FRAME_CSP` is `default-src 'none'; script-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; img-src 'self' data: blob:; connect-src 'none'; sandbox allow-scripts; frame-ancestors 'self'; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'none'`.
`'self'` may not match the opaque origin; a separate script file may not load. That is not "fixed" by adding
`allow-same-origin`. It was not measured in a browser (the `SP_E2E` test is written and not run here).

**Personal switches.** 设置 → 「界面插件」 (`sp.pref.ui-plugins`) lists the server's loaded ui plugins of scope
`personal`. The switch is this browser's `localStorage`. It does not call the server and does not affect other
players. The host may 「推荐」 (`sp.pref.ui-recommend`); the room panel shows 「房主推荐」. [ASSUMED] the mark is local
even during a match, because it changes no match. Another client does not see it until a later phase puts the same
ids on the existing `room.state` (read already, if present, from `room.uiRecommend`, `room.content.uiRecommend`,
`room.state.uiRecommend` or `room.state.content.uiRecommend`). No new protocol message, and `server/lobby.js` is
unchanged.

**Errors and the schema.** `PLUGIN_SLOT`, `PLUGIN_RATE`, `PLUGIN_STORAGE`, `PLUGIN_INTENT`, `PLUGIN_SHAPE` and
`PLUGIN_LIMIT` stay in `shared/plugins/ui.js`. They are merged into `shared/plugins/errors.js` at integration (C6).
`schemas/ui.schema.json` describes the plugin→host messages (C7). Sections §11–§13 of PLUGINS.md stay reserved for
P1b, P1c and P2; the reference for this phase is §14.

### 32.9 The plugin studio and the authoring tools

The owner's decision of 2026-10-13 (the 0.3.0 plan): every authoring tool of the design study's §8 ships with 0.3.0, the
first part here — someone who does not write code makes, previews and packs a cosmetic plugin in the browser; someone who
does gets a schema, findings in plain words and hot reload — and the packer does **not** screen for official game files
(what a plugin carries is its author's responsibility; PLUGINS.md §1 still asks never to put them in). The data, DSL,
script and interface templates and the battle test bench are the second part (work package J), added as modules. With no
plugin and the studio never opened nothing changes: the pages, the served manifests, `welcome`, `/healthz`, the room
states and the golden results are those of §32.3. The reference is PLUGINS.md §15; the beginner's guide (Chinese)
PLUGIN-GUIDE.md; the guide for AI agents `plugins/AGENTS.md`.

**The error catalogue** (`shared/plugins/errors.js`, the plan's interface C6): every `PLUGIN_*` code — the five room
refusals of §32.3 (ERR codes, translated by the packs) and 64 codes of the checks and the studio — with `{ zh, en, fix:
{ zh, en }, doc }`, a level, a symptom for the tables and the studio's one-click fix where one exists. The checks keep
their English `problems` / `warnings` text unchanged (the logs, the tests and the tools read it) and return beside it the
same findings as `issues` — `{ level, code, text, at?, file?, params }`, `at` the path in the manifest (`['assets',
'chars', …]` for the overlay), `params` what the explanation names (`issueList()`; a check that predates the catalogue
is read as `PLUGIN_INVALID` with its text: `issuesOf`) — through `shared/plugins/manifest.js` (manifest 1 and 2, the
files), `shared/plugins/visual.js` (the overlay, the lock), `server/plugins/registry.js` (the scan: skipped entries and
warnings carry their issues; the start log adds the plain words of a skipped plugin's first problem) and
`server/plugins/pack.js`. The symptom tables of PLUGINS.md §15 (English) and PLUGIN-GUIDE.md (Chinese) are generated from
it (`node tools/plugin.mjs errors --write`). A page shows the Chinese text in a Chinese interface (zh, zh-TW) and the
English one in the others [ASSUMED: C6 defines two languages; the studio's own words are translated by the four packs].

**Schemas** (the plan's interface C7): `schemas/plugin.schema.json` (manifest 2: the fields, the class rules for scope,
parts, entry points and permissions, every part's permission, every permission used) and `schemas/overlay.schema.json`
(the asset overlay: the containers, records, files by kind, Spine models, tracks, references), JSON Schema 2020-12 —
accepted exactly when the checks find neither a problem nor a warning, on one fixture set (`test/plugins/schema.test.js`,
with a small validator of its own: `test/helpers/jsonSchema.js`); what only the checks see is named in their
descriptions (§15). The schema is stricter where the checks silently ignore a value of another type.

**The packer** moves into the server (`server/plugins/pack.js`: the folder's check, the lock with each Spine model's clip
data — `skeletonClipData`, also the preview's —, `packPlugin`, `zipPlugin`; `tools/plugin.mjs` re-exports
`checkPluginFolder` and `buildPluginLock`), because the release zips ship `server/` and not `tools/plugin.mjs`
(`tools/assets/` ships). `server/plugins/zip.js` writes deterministic zips (entries sorted, one fixed time, media stored,
the rest deflated; no ZIP64 — a plugin is ≤ 200 MB) — a zip can be pinned by its SHA-256 (the catalogue of a later step).
This closes §32.4's "a zip from tools/plugin.mjs pack".

**The tool** (`tools/plugin.mjs`): `check` prints the plain words under each technical line (`--lang zh|en`, default
Chinese [ASSUMED]; `--json`: every issue with its code, place, params, explanation, fix, document and one-click fix — for
editors and agents); `pack --zip [--out]` (default `plugins/_dist/<id>-<version>.zip`, a `_` folder the registry never
reads); `new <template> <id> [--target <官方名字|id>] [--name] [--author] [--license] [--dir]` (the studio's templates; a
target found by its official name); `errors [--markdown] [--write]`.

**The studio** (`server/studio/`, `public/studio/`, the plan's interface C5): the page `/studio/` and its API
`/studio/api/*` (the routes: PLUGINS.md §15), answered before the request listener's method check (the API takes POST,
PUT, DELETE) and only for this machine and the local network — `server/studio/gate.js`: the socket's peer and every
address any forwarding header names loopback or private (`isLocalIp`; stricter than `clientAddress`, which picks one
header: a header may refuse, never admit), the Host a local address or a local name (DNS rebinding, a public address), a
change with `X-SP-Studio: 1` and the studio's origin; anything else gets the 404 page of a page that does not exist, at
every route and method. `SP_STUDIO=local|off`, `SP_STUDIO_HOSTS` [ASSUMED: on for the local network by default, the names
allowed]. A raw TCP tunnel on the machine (frp tcp, ssh -R) looks like the machine itself and a client that is not a
browser writes any Host: every change also carries **the studio's key** (`server/studio/key.js`: 128 random bits kept in
`plugins/.studio-key`, the same across restarts; the boot banner prints the studio's address with it, `…/studio/#key=…`;
the page keeps it in localStorage and asks for it when it has none; a wrong one → 403 `PLUGIN_STUDIO_KEY`), and the
studio counts as opened (`activate`: the watch, the rooms' rule) only from a request with the key [ASSUMED: the key file;
reads — the page, the list, a plugin's files — need none]. A plugin's files reach the page only of the types the studio
writes and sandboxed (a page or a script put in a folder by hand never runs in the server's origin); the page is never
framed by another (X-Frame-Options, frame-ancestors).
Every file it touches is inside `plugins/<id>/` (`server/studio/files.js`: plugin ids only, plain paths, the class's file
types, no link inside the folder followed — for reading or writing, also by the packer (`plainPath`), the lock and the
studio's note renamed over a link put in their place —, a plugin folder that is itself a link never opened (403; the
registry may still load it and serves only the files its overlay names), every walk bounded (`WALK_LIMITS`: 2000 folders, 12
levels, 50 000 entries; a folder past them refused), a file system error answered with its code only, atomic writes, 64 MB per
file, 1 MB for plugin.json, 160 MB per request, the plugin limits [ASSUMED: the studio's limits]). It makes a plugin from a
template (`server/studio/templates/`: art, spine, voice, audio — one module each; `plugin.json` with `$schema`, version
1.0.0, the minor line of this release as `app` [ASSUMED], both scopes, `assets.override`, the target's empty entry; a
README; nothing else). The `dsl` template is a content plugin (`scope` room only, permissions `data.add` and `dsl`):
it writes one operator whose kit is a passive DSL spec and one prep file under `dsl/`, and it takes no cosmetic target.
The studio finds targets by their official names (`server/studio/targets.js`: the game data and the art
manifest; operators, summons, enemies, items, bonds, strategies, interface pictures, music, sounds), puts dropped files
into a slot under the slot's names (a model's skeleton and atlas under one name, its pages by theirs, `textures` from the
atlas), borrows the installation's art (a reference), shows the server's own findings beside their place with one-click
fixes (`server/studio/edit.js`: permissions, `manifest` / `version` / `app` / `api` / `scope`, unknown fields, writes that
cannot resolve, a model's pages, the lock; a file of a type the plugin may not carry moved to `plugins/_moved/<id>/`,
never deleted [ASSUMED]), previews the plugin as it is now beside the original with the game's own renderer and
components (the battlefield view in the preparation phase and in a battle with every role — fed as a battle feeds it —, the
card picture and the detail card's portrait, the voices and sounds; the temporary composition `server/studio/preview.js`
reads a model's clips from the skeleton when it is not packed yet) and packs it (lock, check, zip download). The page keeps
what it shows of a plugin in the dot file `.studio.json` (never listed, served, checked or packed) [ASSUMED].

**Hot reload** (`server/studio/watch.js`, the design study's §8.3): from the studio's first request (`activate`) the
plugins folder is watched (fs.watch over the tree, 300 ms [ASSUMED]) and every studio write rescans at once; the registry's
listeners follow (§32.3.1 onStale), and `server/lobby.js` — its only change for the studio: `autoApplyPlugins` (a rule
the studio sets) and `refreshPlugins(room)` — applies a room's same plugins again by itself when the studio's rule names
the room: its host is the author (a game session the page named with this browser's `sp.tokens`, `POST
/studio/api/authors`, for 10 minutes after it last did [ASSUMED]; a room such a host already holds applies a change it was
told about at once) or its host is its only player. During a match too: a cosmetic set never changes a battle (the `cs`
stays the vanilla one), the members follow `room.state.content.vs` as they do (§32.3.1: what is drawn follows), nobody is
un-readied; such a room follows every rescan that changes one of its plugins (the studio looks again after each one: the
visual sets tell a held set once, and a plugin broken for a moment while its author saves it, then mended, is followed
too). Only a room of cosmetic plugins applies a change by itself (integration with §32.6): a room with gameplay plugins —
or one whose plugins would now make a content set — would get another `cs` (every page loads and checks it again, the
players are un-readied, a match keeps its set), so its host is told instead and re-applies from the panel.
Any other room's host is told as in §32.3.1; a server whose owner never opens the studio keeps §32.3's
behaviour exactly (no watch, every host told). The page hears the plugins whose state changed through server-sent events
(`GET /studio/api/events`). A player's personal plugins follow on the next catalogue fetch [not pushed: a later step].

**Interface words** — the studio's strings go through `t()` and are in the four language packs; `tools/i18n.mjs` and
`test/i18n.test.js` scan `public/studio/` beside `public/js/`, and ESLint lints it as browser code.

[ASSUMED] — the choices of this step, for the owner to confirm or change: the studio on for the local network by default
(`SP_STUDIO=local|off`) and the local names a Host may have; the studio's limits; the catalogue's explanations in Chinese
for zh / zh-TW and in English for the other interfaces; `check` in Chinese unless `--lang en`; a new plugin's `app` the
release's minor line; the studio's key in `plugins/.studio-key` and reads without it; a plugin folder that is itself a link
never opened by the studio; the walk limits (2000 folders, 12 levels, 50 000 entries) and `plugins/` watched as a tree up to
100 000 entries; the hot reload quiet time (300 ms)
and the author's session kept 10 minutes; a room applies a change by itself also during a match; a disallowed file moved
to `plugins/_moved/`, never deleted; the sidecar `.studio.json`; the studio's slot names (art/<key>/<field>,
spine/<key>/<side>/, voice/<key>/<dub>/<line>, music/<key>/, sound/…); the voice line labels; the templates' scope (both)
and permission (`assets.override`); the schemas' stricter types.

Tests: `test/plugins/errors.test.js`, `test/plugins/schema.test.js`, `test/plugins/tool.test.js` (new, plain words,
`--json`, `pack --zip`, `errors`), `test/studio/studio.test.js`, `test/studio/hotreload.test.js`, the browser test
`test/ui/studio.e2e.test.js` (`SP_E2E=1`); still green: `test/plugins/*`, `test/lobby-plugins.test.js` (P1a's notice
unchanged without the studio), `test/i18n.test.js` (now over `public/studio/` too), the golden results (no scenario moved).

#### 32.9.1 Gameplay authoring, trial bench and room settings (WP-J)

The owner's WP-J task extends C5 and completes the first-wave integration note 7. The template and editor modules add
numbers, operator, DSL, prep meta, mode, UI and script wizards, shared with `tools/plugin.mjs new` (`--options` accepts a
JSON file). The operator wizard resolves official kit keys by name, creates namespaced compact records (normal/elite),
and uses art references. DSL uses WP-A2's `kit.dsl`, manifest `dsl` files, permission and schema. The default template preserves A2's compact caster and income file, with the structured editor and five additional examples on the same build contract.
The structured editor uses PLUGIN-DSL §7's five examples and the real validator, with C6 explanations. The script
wizard includes the WP-F harness and is read-only in the browser. The UI preview reuses `pluginFrame.js` with fake
state and volatile storage; sandbox attributes, navigation shutdown, message validation and limits stay the game's.

Authoring state sits in the existing hidden sidecar, with a revision of the folder's bytes. Saves reject an external
edit and preserve unowned metadata/settings/dependencies. Executable files are written only by the fixed template
builders; generic studio PUT/GET never exposes an executable document. UI frames load via the sandboxed registry route.

The trial service forks one disposable process per comparison. That process checks the same folder and C1 content set,
then plays the original followed by the plugin using the same seed/scenario. It returns actual BattleSpec, field meta,
snapshots/events, counts, damage statistics and results. The browser consumes the game's wire shapes with the game
renderer, never imports plugin code, and can pause/replay/change speed. Unsaved wizard drafts use temporary copies.
Script execution requires the server's enabled snapshot, mode and matching fresh lock; native execution retains the owner pin gate. The studio key is not a bypass.
A PLUGIN_* failure returns plain findings and no battle. A stalled job is killed and frees its slot; shutdown kills jobs.

[ASSUMED] beyond the task's interface:

- Two concurrent trials, 20 wall seconds per job (including validation/composition), 512 MB child heap; 256 KB requests,
  16 MB returned message, 6 MB replay per side. Presets run 1–120 game seconds; source-checkout golden battle cases
  (not match cases) are capped at 240 game seconds. Three ticks per captured snapshot/event frame, as the game watcher.
- The dummy is a stationary non-attacking enemy with 1,000,000 HP. The synthetic lane and adjacent positions are test
  fixtures only. New operators compare to their borrowed official reference, or Texas if none resolves, explicitly named.
- The two-operator exercise uses the engine's forceAttack and ally-target API with existing attack cadence/skill stats,
  ends at the first knock-out and does not alter camp rules. Enemy-only skill selectors remain enemy-only. It is labelled
  a stationary attack exercise, not an official PvP mode. Damage in this exercise comes from actual damaged hooks because
  the normal opposing-camp damage statistic deliberately excludes friendly damage. Prep is not dispatched by a battle.
- UI previews use fake public/private state and one fake damage batch per second; preview storage is memory-only.

The room panel sends boolean/integer/number/typed-enum settings through existing `room.setPlugins`, normalized by C1;
nonempty normalized settings are carried on the resolved plugin entries for all viewers. Reapply and toggles retain them;
editing an automatic dependency makes it explicit. No setting field is added when no plugin uses one. `contentSets`
adds registry-backed `onStale`; visual and content notices have independent keys per room composition. Only the host is told, once per kind, with pending names merged after an active match. The old set remains immutable until host reapply, which uses the existing content-ready and
script consent flow. The studio watch also compares content/script/UI hashes. The vanilla room and empty-content bytes
remain identical to P0 computed from this same checkout; the shared C6 catalogue edit naturally changes the build hash.
No golden scenario changes and no generated data or game assets are edited.

References updated: PLUGINS §11/§15, PLUGIN-GUIDE (new operator / DSL talent / try a battle), plugins/AGENTS.md,
design/network.md §8.4 and design/client.md. Tests: `test/studio/gameplay.test.js`, `trial.test.js`, `room-settings.test.js`;
existing schema/tool/error/client regressions; the two opt-in browser files `studio-gameplay.e2e.test.js` and
`plugins-settings.e2e.test.js`. Browser coverage requires Chrome and a host able to listen on localhost. All template strict/composition assertions run after the A2 merge, including a composed DSL unit and a generated prep effect in a real match-host round. No core sim file is changed.

WP-J2 integration and review repairs: the parent owns draft `sp-studio-trial-*` directories. Its finish path kills the detached process group (POSIX SIGKILL; Windows `taskkill /T /F`), waits for the writer to exit, and removes the directory on success, validation refusal, timeout, crash, IPC failure, close and client disconnect. The child deletes before sending, too. Normal HTTP request-body close is not a cancellation; an incomplete request or a response closed before completion is. Probe descendants keep the shared `scriptPreflight` SIGKILL deadline, 5 s by default / `SP_SCRIPT_PROBE_MS`.

[ASSUMED] A separate simulation child consumes the built set and scenes. Sandbox mode uses `sandboxProcessOptions`, initializes SES before importing engine code, and calls the formal host's `installSandboxScripts`; trusted mode uses `loadScripts` after the same lock/pin admission. Both children remain inside the trial's process group and heap limit; the sandbox simulation has no filesystem write or child-creation permission. Replays returned to the page are data. Incompatible sandbox syntax is refused and is never elevated to trusted execution.

Script source in GET plugin state now requires the studio key. Numeric controls use the shared `officialBbRange`/`dataBound`, and the generator and folder checker call `appendOfficialBounds` on patched official values (dependency-only paths still require full C1). DSL new/save validates generated specs before writing; save rechecks current frontend validation, and network failure has an explicit message. Draft writes pass `isPackPath` and reject absolute paths. The six authoring/trial routes and six WP-J error codes are intentionally added to the frozen stable list; Forge/catalogue routes and both UI entries remain.

Regressions: `test/studio/review.test.js` covers all cleanup exits, request/response close, real pinned SIGTERM-ignoring probes, actual sandbox/native kits, no native fallback, draft paths, shared maximum/positive minimum checks and refused DSL saves. Room settings tests distinguish visual/content notices. Browser coverage adds DSL creation/trial and pack after replay; the opt-in suites require Chrome and a listening server.

#### 32.9.2 Release author resources and Docker plugin storage

Full/lite releases and updates include `schemas/`, `tools/plugin.mjs`, the four plugin references and catalogue
example, `plugins/README.md`, `plugins/AGENTS.md`, the tracked `_template/` tree and `test/helpers/battleHarness.js`.
The harness imports only shipped server/shared code; the rest of the development tests stays out. The packager uses
an explicit allowlist for plugin resources, even if a server owner's plugin or backup was accidentally tracked.
The source-only trial import of `tools/golden.mjs` is the one optional packaging import: release trial options omit
those cases and the dispatcher rejects them when absent. Production installation builds real `node_modules` and
vendors SES/Acorn before writing the release manifest. Update comparison includes every added/changed file and
records removed baseline files in `UPDATE.json`; the existing hash-checked boot cleanup is unchanged.

Docker stores bundled plugin resources at `/app/plugin-resources`, separate from the writable `/app/plugins` volume.
`SP_PLUGIN_RESOURCES` selects that resource root for `server/studio/gameData.js`; unset, templates are read from the
installation's `plugins/_template/` as before. [ASSUMED] This environment name and the image's resource path are
installation choices. Resources remain root-owned; the writable plugins directory belongs to the `node` user.
The image includes the CLI, schemas, docs and harness, and installs actual production dependency files under its
installation root so permission-limited sandbox children need no external node_modules symlink.

The script wizard writes its id into the original manifest, as required by descriptor verification. Previously the
generated manifest omitted it: folder checks passed but composition/author tests refused it.

Validation: package selection/stage checks, offline staged CLI new/check/pack/test and trial execution, and a 0.2.6
update round trip; Docker context/resource checks. No gameplay or golden change.

### 32.10 P3a — unified SDK and trusted execution

Sources: the maintainer's 2026-10-13 plan C2/C3, the adopted mod-system study §3.4, §3.6, §5.1–5.4,
§5.7, §7.6 and §8.1–8.2; the existing SIM §5–7, META §2, and §32.5–32.7 contracts. This is new plugin
infrastructure and original template behavior, not a change to official rules. PLUGINS §13 is the API reference.

The extension registry is empty on vanilla. Kit, enemy, battle-module and prep registrations carry their host plugin
id and execute in resolved composition order. Disposable children evaluate each plugin's synchronous setup; cached
registration reports combine in load order to reject duplicate ids before producing a composition; the main server never imports author code. Actual matches install the pinned source
snapshot in their match child before importing Match; browsers install the same snapshot through loadBrowserSim.
Composition descriptors and the original entry bytes are covered by cs. Bot fights and verification share the same
registrations. Script rooms always use a child process, including when data rooms opt into worker threads.

sp:sdk@1 provides registration, the documented tier1 subset, deterministic math, constants, frozen settings and bounded
logging. The registry and CLI share lexical portability checking; sandboxable is not a security guarantee. P3a is
native trusted execution. Owner mode, exact lock/file verification and listening-address checks gate loading. A room's
humans additionally consent one seat at a time for its current cs; seat reconnect preserves consent and cs changes
clear it. Browser consent is local too. Spectators who have not consented download data only and never run scripts.
The dialog, seats and persistent red banner have all four translations. Trusted activate(api) server extensions use
another child and IPC copies for namespaced HTTP routes, read-only lobby events and health data; page entries are
checked ES modules loaded after acceptance/reload.

[ASSUMED] choices (also marked in code/API documentation):

- Manifest overrides authorizes only listed official kit ids; duplicate plugin registrations still fail. Normal/elite
  aliases conflict. Plugin char_x_ ids support normal shop and DIY dispatch; official lookup stays unchanged.
- Synchronous setup is preflighted in a disposable child during composition, before player consent (registration only,
  no match). Timeout: 5 s per uncached plugin by default (`SP_SCRIPT_PROBE_MS`, 500–60000 — integration: 1 s killed
  honest sandboxed plugins under load, the child starting the engine and SES alone needed more), using SIGKILL; spawnSync waits/reaps the child
  before returning, so even setup that catches SIGTERM and loops is terminated. Both successful and failed probes
  are cached by plugin id + lock hash + descriptor hash + normalized settings, at most 256 recent results per registry.
  Settings may change registration ids; the descriptor distinguishes actual source bytes even in unpinned CLI tests.
  Cached reports still undergo cross-plugin conflict checks, including normal/elite aliases. After eviction a probe
  runs again. The owner has already explicitly authorized native code; players authorize page code.
  **Residual risk: the lobby pauses during an uncached probe**, including vanilla rooms; multiple cold plugins add
  their pauses. Async spawn plus a timer would avoid that pause, but changing synchronous C1 also changes contentSets /
  roomSets.resolve, lobby selection and studio/CLI callers. This repair keeps C1 synchronous; an asynchronous C1 migration
  needs coordinated work with the content/room packages. The probe ceiling bounds setup, not the rest of data composition.
- Default script budget is DSL's 8000. Each wrapped callback and engine action counts, per plugin per battle. Prep hooks
  have a separate 8000-call counter per match round. Prior statuses/summons and active skill mods follow DSL cleanup
  semantics. Direct engine-object mutations remain available to kit hooks in both modes. The Worker/child watchdog in §32.11
  interrupts nonreturning simulation code; native trusted page code remains able to block its page.
- trusted-force is the explicit public-bind override. Wildcard addresses are public for this gate even though isLocalIp
  treats unspecified peer addresses as local. net.isIP validates literals; numeric checks reject IPv4 0.0.0.0/8,
  all spellings of all-zero IPv6, and mapped equivalents. Mapped local IPv4 addresses remain local. After listen succeeds,
  server.address().address is checked again: refusal disables scripts (also after a rescan) and awaits SIGKILL/exit of
  any already started trusted service. A public reverse proxy requires the same trusted-force decision even on a
  loopback listener; this gate cannot discover proxy exposure. Trust policy changes do not revoke running snapshots.
- Script descriptors are JSON carrying the original source and manifest, hashed as files['@script']; verified source
  uses native data-URL import on Node, or the verified same-origin browser routes of §32.11 with the SDK specifier linked. Entries are self-contained; test files may import helpers.
- Log limit 20 lines per setup, 500 characters per value; CLI plugin tests time out at 120 s. The explicit local
  `tools/plugin.mjs test` command ignores server mode and the trusted pin; its authorization is the command itself,
  not a claim that tested files match a production pin. Its behavior is unchanged by the review repair.
- Experimental server activation uses one child with a 128 MB V8 old-space heap (not total RSS or a descendant-process
  limit); activation and route answers time out at 5 s. Timeout, startup failure and close use SIGKILL and await exit,
  clearing routes/health and failing pending requests. All server entries in that child stop together. Health JSON is
  limited to 16 KiB UTF-8 per plugin in both the API and parent IPC receiver; unknown ids / oversized IPC updates are
  ignored. Routes return JSON and receive method/path/url only. The parent independently validates each IPC route's
  loaded plugin id and exact /x/<id>/ prefix, rejects .., case-insensitive %2e and backslashes, and compares the same raw
  path as the HTTP dispatcher, never a URL-normalized pathname. Nonmatches retain the original health/static handling.
  Lobby event copies contain public room state, never reconnect tokens. Server
  entry changes require restart. The server owner authorizes standalone server entries; page consent is still per room.
- Browser choice persists under sp.plugin.consent.<cs>, with a page-local fallback if storage is unavailable. Accepted
  trusted.page entries reload with cs and room parameters. Reload is necessary to remove arbitrary native side effects.

Tests added: script.test.js, script-consent.test.js, script-integration.test.js, and plugins-script.e2e.test.js. They
cover lexical denials, SDK exposure, registration ordering/conflicts, owner gates, per-seat consent/reconnect/cs change,
budget cutoff, source hash verification, unconsenting spectator downloads, actual child vs loadBrowserSim bit identity,
a complete four-bot match, trusted server IPC and the template's CLI harness. Golden: all 287 existing scenarios match;
no golden files move. Browser/HTTP tests require a host allowed to listen on a loopback port.

Review repair regressions: script-review.test.js and script-routes-review.test.js cover every reported zero-address
spelling and mapped/private addresses, actual-bind rechecking and rescan persistence, SIGTERM-catching setup and service
callbacks (PID absent after cleanup), cached probe failures/settings/lock changes/conflicts, health UTF-8 byte limits,
forged route IPC, raw HTTP traversal and the trusted client .js diagnostic. script-integration.test.js also verifies
that the explicit author test still runs with a mismatching pin and sandbox server mode.


### 32.11 P3b — Worker simulation, SES and CSP

This supersedes §32.6's browser execution model: **every non-vanilla gameplay set** (data, DSL or script) gets a
module `WorkerSimHost` per local battle. Vanilla and cosmetic-only rooms retain `InPageSimHost`; they never import
SES or call lockdown. `runner.js` owns pacing, pause, background pumping, catch-up slices and reporting. The C4 API is
`load(cs, content?) / start(spec, opts) / step(n, opts?) → { snap, ev, state } / unitStats(id) / ownerOps(pid) / stop()`;
Worker operations are asynchronous. The page passes checked data and the official `bounds` snapshot by postMessage;
the Worker installs it through `installOfficialBounds`, never scans modified data for its own caps and never fetches
JSON. `simLoader.js` retains the original loader and `runner.js` re-exports its old public names. Worker state contains
wire snapshots, field meta, progress, LP meter, owner/operator records and requested live stats, never a Battle instance.
A details read returns the last cached stats and schedules a fresh read; it never recomputes `unit.s`.

[ASSUMED] A Worker RPC without a reply for **2000 ms** (including install, start and forceEnd, which can execute plugin
code) terminates that Worker. `WorkerSimHost({ timeoutMs })` / runner dependency `workerTimeoutMs` configures it.
[ASSUMED] Trusted module/data loading has a separate 15 s timeout (`loadTimeoutMs`); a separate install RPC starts
the 2 s deadline before any plugin code executes. A parallel test caught cold module loading exceeding 2 s and
triggering a false takeover, which motivated this split. Only one batch per battle is outstanding. Clearing the round, superseding a pending replica or evicting an entry
terminates its Worker and rejects outstanding calls; late replies cannot resurrect it. The page immediately stops
progress/result reporting and shows `插件卡死，本场由服务器接管` (all four packs). [ASSUMED] To reuse the existing
protocol without a new C2S intent, a failing gameplay authority sends a structurally valid empty forced `b.result`
**without cs**: §32.6's mandatory-content refusal invokes the existing server takeover/handover. This signal never
claims a simulated result, is never sent by vanilla or a display replica, and the ordinary deadline still covers a
lost connection. Successful results retain the simulation's own `cs`. The server host's existing heartbeat watchdog
ends that content set's matches and calls the lobby's onEnd path.

**Script boundary.** `shared/plugins/sandbox.js` initializes SES 2.3.0 with `errorTaming: 'safe'` and
`stackFiltering: 'concise'` before importing the engine. Safe error taming clears stacks in the host as well as the
guest; the deprecated/no-effect mathTaming and dateTaming options are omitted. Each plugin has one Compartment.
The only application capability is hardened `sp`. Its settings/constants and SDK-owned record results are deeply
frozen copies; target-list containers are frozen while their live Unit elements remain mutable. Battle/Unit and hook
handles are not hardened: kit installation and hooks must modify engine state. `harden` and `lockdown` are explicitly
shadowed as undefined in guest globals. This is capability isolation, not a read-only membrane around the engine.
The compartment Math is a frozen null-prototype copy: basic deterministic operations/constants plus detmath
sin/cos/atan2/hypot/powi, no random or other engine-approximated functions. [ASSUMED] arguments must be finite;
hypot takes two arguments, pow an integer exponent (safe integer) and |base| ≤ 2^500, so detmath's native-pow fallback
cannot run. Date, performance, Intl, WeakRef, FinalizationRegistry, SharedArrayBuffer, Atomics, fetch, timers,
queueMicrotask, process, require and Compartment are unavailable. [ASSUMED] eval, Function and Promise are also
removed so generated source/microtasks cannot bypass the parsed synchronous surface. `globalThis` is SES's isolated
global, not the host. Built-in prototypes are frozen; prototype-constructor escapes throw.

The source adapter uses Acorn 8.18.0, evaluating transformed module bodies **only inside SES**, never host eval.
Only `sp:sdk@1` (exports `sp` and `default`) and own relative `.js`/`.mjs` paths resolve. Every supplied file's exact
UTF-8 bytes must match SHA-256; the whole reachable graph is checked before evaluation. [ASSUMED] limits: 64 source
files / 1 MiB per plugin; no cyclic imports, export-star, mutable export declarations, destructuring exports,
import.meta, dynamic import, asynchronous/generator source or exponentiation syntax. Imports are initialization-time
bindings; shared module namespaces/exports are hardened, so authors should keep mutable state inside callbacks.
These restrictions are explicit validation failures, not a trusted-execution fallback. They are a first adapter
surface, not a promise of full native ESM semantics. WP-F's deterministic callback/action budgets remain the SDK's
responsibility; arbitrary loops are stopped by the host watchdog, not a wall-clock budget inside the sim.

**Integrated execution.** Both executors consume `scripts/<id>.json`, verified by `verifyScripts` against the
composition's `files['@script']`, manifest and original entry hashes. The descriptor's `execution` is `sandbox` or
`trusted`, so changing mode changes cs and invalidates old consent. Sim/meta entries of a plugin share one SDK and
one Compartment; duplicate entry paths execute once, in resolved plugin order. `installSandboxScripts` adapts the
verified source strings to the Acorn/Compartment loader, calls `createSDK(plugin, { settings, log })`, then executes
`export default function setup(sp)` and seals `server/sim/content/extensions.js`. Registration is transactional.
The shared SDK named imports (`kit`, `math`, `constants`) work identically in both modes. The existing deterministic
scriptGuard/DSL budget also applies inside SES. Production manifests currently allow self-contained entry modules;
relative-module support in the low-level sandbox loader does not broaden that manifest contract.

[ASSUMED, pending maintainer confirmation] `SP_PLUGIN_SCRIPTS=sandbox` enables only script-class plugins with a
valid lock and matching files; it needs no trusted hash pin, allows public listeners, and requires no per-player
consent. The room shows `脚本（沙箱）` and remains unofficial. `trusted` / `trusted-force` retain §32.10's native import,
pinning, address gates and individual consent. A composition containing trusted-class code is entirely trusted and
needs consent for every script member; sandbox mode cannot enable trusted-class plugins and never falls back to
native code. All gameplay simulations still use browser Workers; trusted page entries alone execute on the page.
Sandbox composition preflight uses the same SES/SDK adapter in a permission-limited child (128 MiB), with §32.10's
5 s default SIGKILL timeout/cache (`SP_SCRIPT_PROBE_MS`). Its synchronous C1 lobby-pause limitation remains. Explicit CLI author tests keep native
execution authorization regardless of the server mode.

**Native browser bytes.** The descriptor also pins `browserSources`: sim/meta source with only `sp:sdk@1` linked
to `/shared/plugins/sdk.js`, and unchanged trusted page source. The server serves exactly these retained bytes at
`/cs/<cs16>/scripts/<id>/<entry>` as `text/javascript`, never reopening plugin files. The page checks the response's
raw bytes against the verified descriptor before importing that same address or passing it to a Worker. The Worker
only imports modules, never fetches JSON or performs a connect request. Each simulation Worker permits one installation; its fresh module realm isolates native module state.
The import uses exactly the URL whose response was checked. No data/blob script URL or page unsafe-eval
allowance is needed. Trusted server/native Node loading retains its existing data-URL implementation.

**Node child.** `SP_PLUGIN_SCRIPTS=sandbox` is required (default off). [ASSUMED] this mode itself has no public-IP gate;
public use remains an owner decision with the risks below. Script sets force process transport even when
`SP_MATCH_HOST=worker`. `forkSandbox` supplies `--permission`, `--allow-fs-read=<install root>` and the existing
old-space cap (default 768 MiB, minimum 64). IPC is JSON; bytes are explicit arrays, reconstituted and hashed in the
child. It does not inherit NODE_OPTIONS or session/environment secrets; only SP_VERIFY, SP_COMBAT and SP_BOT_EMOTES
are copied as explicit match policy. Tests on **Node 22.22.0, macOS** confirmed
SES+JSON IPC, denied reads of /etc/hosts, denied file writes and process creation, a 2 s killed infinite setup, and
an allocation bomb aborting the child alone. A host integration test checks the real heartbeat termination/onEnd
path using the production SDK, as do the child/Worker/native determinism and budget tests.

Node permissions are defence in depth, **not** a security boundary against malicious native/escaped code; Node 22
does not restrict network, existing file descriptors bypass file permissions, and installation symlinks enlarge the
read surface (including npm-link or a linked node_modules tree). Do not place links to outside files inside the
installation root. Run under a low-privilege account and preferably a container/network namespace with network/memory/CPU restrictions
[ASSUMED deployment recommendation]. A browser Worker isolates a blocked JS loop, not browser-process memory
exhaustion; a SES escape still reaches same-origin IndexedDB even though CSP blocks fetch. Engine/SES flaws and
intentional divergent results remain risks; verification and OS isolation are still needed.

**Parent IPC.** Both host/index and MatchProxy validate child messages before delivery. [ASSUMED] log text is at
most 4000 UTF-8 bytes, boot/failed error text 300, reply/end JSON 1 MiB, and send/broadcast envelopes 16 MiB;
JSON traversal is bounded to depth 64 and 500,000 values. Message types, log levels, match ids, recipient/request ids,
plain JSON shapes and match-only m.*/b.* frame types are checked. Invalid messages are ignored; pending calls retain
their watchdog/timeout. These checks happen after Node decodes IPC: a compromised child can still consume parent
memory during decoding, flood many valid packets or falsify its own set's gameplay. OS isolation remains necessary.

**CSP.** Pages get `default-src 'self'; script-src 'self' 'sha256-<importmap>'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'
https://fonts.googleapis.com; font-src 'self' https://fonts.gstatic.com; img-src 'self' data: blob:;
connect-src 'self'; worker-src 'self' blob:; frame-src 'self'; object-src 'none'; base-uri 'none'`.
All shipped import maps are hashed from their exact body bytes at startup. Boot diagnostics, nomodule fallback,
font load handling and the UI kit module are external scripts. `/studio/` gets the same policy. [ASSUMED] `SP_CSP=off`
disables the site policy; the simulation Worker and UI-frame response policies remain enforced independently.
`/p/` UI frames keep §32.8's exact policy. The simulation Worker's actual file (encoded/case aliases included), on
200/206/304, carries `default-src 'none'; script-src 'self' 'unsafe-eval'; connect-src 'none'`. The Worker
alone permits eval for SES; its parent never does. The review's Node --disallow-code-generation-from-strings probe
confirmed that lockdown/Compartment construction can succeed without eval but evaluate('1+1') fails. The browser
control now asserts that evaluation actually returns 2, and rejects the same RPC under the no-eval policy.
The page's connect-src is same-origin only, including its existing same-origin WebSocket; arbitrary ws:/wss: schemes
are removed. Browser regression tests cover the actual two-player connection.

The installed PixiJS 7.4.2 shader path uses `new Function`; the app now explicitly installs the upstream MIT
`@pixi/unsafe-eval` 7.4.2 static-uniform adapter before creating a renderer (transpiled source with its provenance
in `pixiNoEval.js`, notice in THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md). Its bitmap loaders also contain blob Worker constructors.
No `new Function` was found in the installed three/spine runtime sources used here. These are source findings and
unit tests, **not browser measurements**: this task environment refuses localhost listen with EPERM. Chrome CSP
inheritance, SES eval behavior, rendered Pixi/three/spine compatibility, the existing real/mock/render/plugins/UI/
studio suites, and Node-vs-Chrome traces are written/attempted but **not run** here. Firefox/Safari are also pending.
Do not treat this as a browser security sign-off.

Validation: original runner regressions (38), targeted content loader checks, shared escape corpus, data Worker/page
packets, script operator permission-child/Worker traces, actual blocked Worker loops, heap limit, host watchdog,
CSP headers without a listener, and static Pixi uniform uploads. Golden: all 287 scenarios unchanged. Worker overhead
is reported with its fixture and measured values in the WP-G report; it is a round-trip cost, not a full-roster budget.
Sources: [SES 2.3.0 source/docs](https://github.com/endojs/endo/tree/master/packages/ses),
[Node 22 permission documentation](https://nodejs.org/docs/latest-v22.x/api/permissions.html),
[PixiJS 7.4.2 no-eval adapter](https://github.com/pixijs/pixijs/tree/v7.4.2/packages/unsafe-eval/src).

### 32.12 Forge pack import

The owner's decision of 2026-10-13 (the 0.3.0 plan, work package H): a Forge workshop pack — the community fork's
format, not its code — can be imported as the plugins it can become. The importer is original. Forge is
GPL-3.0-or-later; nothing was copied from it, so `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES` gains no row. The importer does not screen
for official art, the same rule as the packer. The reference is PLUGINS.md §16. The beginner's steps are
PLUGIN-GUIDE.md, 「从 Forge 包导入」.

**The tool** (`server/plugins/forgeImport.js`, `tools/plugin.mjs import-forge <dir|zip> [--out] [--id] [--dry-run]
[--json]`): reads `pack.json` and the files it names. Skins, portraits, avatars, icons and Spine become a cosmetic
overlay, a `{ ref }` when that is what the overlay already uses. Voice, music and sound effects become audio in that
overlay when the key is one this game already has: `voices` to `audio.voiceJp`, `voiceLangs.cn` to `audio.voice`,
bgm tracks `lobby|prep|combat|unite|boss`, `bossBgm` as `{ loop }`, and `sfx.ui` / `sfx.battle` / `sfx.units` in the
overlay's shape. Numeric edits, new operators, new chess pairs (round 43, below) and new enemies with an official
base become a content plugin (patches and compact records). A skill script is never executed by the importer: the
operator gets kit `"generic"` unless a JSON object passes `validateSkillSpec` (`kit.spec`) or its skills are an
official operator's (round 43), and `kits/` become a sandboxed script plugin (round 43). No DSL part is written. A
flat UI translation becomes a lang plugin.
A version-1 content translation is kept only when every leaf replaces an existing string. Announcements and credits
become `description` and `credits`. A `client.panels` entry becomes a ui stub only for `screen.game.hud` and
`screen.game.aside` (`hud.right`) and `screen.result.footer` (`result.tab`), and only when it does not wrap a
component and is not marked read-only false. The stub tells the author to rewrite it to postMessage API v1. The
panel script is not copied. `stats.tab` and `room.panel` have no Forge mount yet. Engine replacement, protocol,
server logic and any script are not imported. Each is one reason in Chinese and in English. There is no
`buildVisualSet`. A cosmetic product is checked with `createVisualSets({ registry }).resolve`, a content product
with `buildContentSet`. A problem or a warning fails the import and the destination is not left.
`IMPORT-REPORT.md` is written into the primary folder after `pack` succeeds.

**The studio.** `/studio/` grows one button, 「从 Forge 包导入」: pick a zip or a folder, read the report, confirm,
and the plugins are written under `plugins/`. While the browser is still reading the file, preview and confirm stay
disabled, the previous report is cleared, and a late read cannot restore it. The request body is `{ zip?: base64, files?: [{ name, data: base64 }],
id? }` (`POST /studio/api/forge/preview`, `POST /studio/api/forge/import`). It is never a path on the server. The
gate and the studio key are the ones §32.9 already requires. The new strings go through `t()` and are in the four
language packs.

**Safety.** Packs are untrusted. The reader is this module's own. It uses `crc32` from `server/plugins/zip.js` and
does not call `unzipFiles`, which has no path or size guard. It rejects path traversal, symbolic links, ZIP64,
encryption, a data descriptor, more files than `PLUGIN_LIMITS.files`, and a declared or inflated size over
`PLUGIN_LIMITS.pluginBytes`. ZIP64 is recognised from the EOCD sentinel, a ZIP64 extra field, or a ZIP64 locator in
the 20 bytes immediately before a real EOCD. A `PK\\x06\\x06` inside an entry is not treated as ZIP64. A raw zip
larger than `PLUGIN_LIMITS.pluginBytes` plus 64 KiB is refused before it is read. JSON uses `checkJsonLimits` and
refuses the keys `__proto__`, `constructor` and `prototype` before any mapping. Asset trees are null-prototype
objects, and a path is walked only through own properties. A directory read uses `lstat`, does not follow a link,
refuses a regular file with `nlink > 1`, opens with `O_NOFOLLOW`, and checks `fstat` before reading. Empty
directories count toward the file cap. A dry run stages under the temporary directory and deletes the stage. A real
write stages in a dot directory beside `plugins/` (the registry skips dot directories) and commits by rename on that
same filesystem. `EXDEV` is a failure: the import does not copy. A failed commit removes only destinations that
attempt created. The stage is always removed. The visual set and the content set are built from a temporary view
that can see plugins already installed and the staged products. The source pack is not linked into that view.

**Errors.** `PLUGIN_FORGE_INPUT`, `PLUGIN_FORGE_ZIP`, `PLUGIN_FORGE_JSON`, `PLUGIN_FORGE_ID`,
`PLUGIN_FORGE_EXISTS` and `PLUGIN_FORGE_SCRIPT` live in `shared/plugins/errors.js` (C6), documented at
`docs/PLUGINS.md#16-forge-import`. The symptom tables are regenerated with `node tools/plugin.mjs errors --write`.

[ASSUMED] — the choices of this step, for the owner to confirm or change: id normalisation and the suffix split
(content keeps the id, cosmetic `<id>-look`, ui `<id>-<slot>`, lang `<id>-<lang>`); record ids use that content id,
including `--out`'s basename; `voices` → `audio.voiceJp` and `voiceLangs.cn` → `audio.voice` (`en` / `kr` have no
table), and those keys are rewritten from the operator and skill id maps, not from the report text; profession
aliases (DEFENDER / HEAVY / GUARDIAN → TANK, VANGUARD → PIONEER, GUARD → WARRIOR) and a missing position; a Forge
form rank is `phase/level/skillLevel/equipLevel` compared as integers, or one integer, and the lowest becomes
normal and the highest elite (one rank copied into both); skills are taken from the selected form when that form
has its own list, otherwise from the record; a missing range grid `[[0, 0]]`; shop tier follows rarity; a 6★
operator declared in `operators` is 自选 only (`as: "diy"`), so no shop piece is written. The compact record
schema still requires `chess.tier`, and that field is the rarity; a declared operator below
6★ is chess; a unit that is not in `operators` is still emitted as chess, unless a `chess.json` pair of the pack is
its shop piece (round 43); an override array is one `set` of the whole array, while `talents` and `talentsBase` (Forge merges those by
index) are named and left unchanged; two i18n entries that canonicalise to one language are refused rather than
merged; every imported file is stored under one extra `files/` prefix so `a.png` and `files/a.png` stay distinct;
a generic kit (since round 43, an official kit or the sandboxed script plugin) instead of executing `kits/*.js`, and
skill fields that do not fit the compact record are named and dropped, not executed; the panel slot map and the read-only heuristic (no `wraps`, and not `readonly` / `readOnly`
false); `skins[]` collapse onto one overlay; `art.audio` is not a documented Forge table, and only bgm, bossBgm and
sfx keys this overlay already has are written; an unreferenced audio file whose basename is a music track is
written to `audio.bgm`; Forge spine `anims` are dropped; an omitted dependency range is `*`; version falls back to
`0.1.0` when the pack version is not a restricted semver; the studio upload is a zip or a file list, not a server
path; staging is a dot directory beside `plugins/` (a dry run uses the temporary directory); the importer does not
screen for official assets; no `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES` row, because no Forge source was copied.

[ASSUMED] residual risks, written down rather than closed: a cross-device rename (`EXDEV`) is refused instead of
copied, so a plugins directory on another volume fails the import; the raw-zip cap is `pluginBytes` plus 64 KiB, so
a legitimate zip only slightly over 200 MB is refused before it is read; refusing `nlink > 1` and re-checking the
open file with `fstat` and `O_NOFOLLOW` reduces a hard-link or a replace-between-stat-and-read, and it does not
cover a filesystem the process is not allowed to stat; the studio body cap bounds the base64 decode, and a second
check of the encoded length (to drop the extra copy sooner) was not added; ZIP directory entries are validated and
not extracted; `tools/plugin.mjs import-forge --out` may write outside `plugins/` because that flag is the
maintainer's choice of folder; modes and new items are still not auto-converted.

Tests: `test/plugins/forgeImport.test.js`, `test/studio/forge.test.js`, and the browser test
`test/ui/forge-import.e2e.test.js` (`SP_E2E=1`; a sandbox that cannot open a browser leaves it unrun). The golden
results do not move.

**Round 43 (the maintainer's approval of 2026-10-15): chess pairs, official kits, kits → a script plugin.** A dry
run of Forge's four example packs showed what the importer left on the floor: `demo-workshop` and `kit-demo` produced
nothing (their `chess.json` records had no mapping), `clementia`'s operator got the generic kit although its skills
are 克莱门莎's official `skchr_clemnt_1..3`, and `kit-demo`'s `kits/chess_ws_abyss_hunter_a.js` was only named. Three
additions, in `server/plugins/forgeImport.js` and the new `server/plugins/forgeKits.js`:

1. **`chess.json` pairs.** A new `_a` / `_b` pair is one compact record: a shop piece, or 自选 when `isDiy` and 6★.
   Normal and elite come from the pair (stats, range, skills, trait, talents); tier, bonds, price, sellPrice,
   garrisons, identifier, shopSortId and the identity and attack fields are kept; the rest is named. The unit is the
   `charId` when that is an official operator or a unit of the pack; with `charId` null, an official operator its
   `assets` name lends the art. A pack unit whose shop piece is a pair is written once, as that pair. The compact
   builder (`shared/plugins/records.js`) gained the smallest field faithful pairs need: `chess.garrisonIds` may be
   `{ normal, golden }`, as `price` already could (official pairs carry `garrison_N_a` / `garrison_N_b`).
2. **Official kits.** When every skill id of a record is an official skill of one operator (`data/chess.json` and
   `data/backups.json` skills → that operator), the record keeps those ids and names that operator's kit with the
   documented `kit: { ref }` — its chess kit (`chess_char_…_a`) for a shop piece with the same default skill, its 自选
   kit (`char_…`, every skill under `skills`) for a 自选 record. Talents and trait are copied (numbers, text, range), so
   the referenced kit reads the record's numbers. Skills now also keep `skillType`, `spType`, `durationType`,
   `trigger`, `maxChargeTime`, `increment`, `descRaw` and their range, which the generic kit reads as well.
3. **Kit scripts → a sandboxed script plugin.** Production script plugins allow only self-contained entries (§32.10:
   `readScripts` reads the entries, the descriptors carry their sources, `scriptCheck` refuses every import but
   `sp:sdk@1`), so a kit is not imported from a copied file. Each `kits/<id>.js` and every `./` file of the pack it
   imports are parsed with acorn and carried in one sim entry, `sim/index.js` of the script plugin `kits-<id>`, one
   function scope per Forge file in dependency order; only the import and export lines change (`@kit/tier1.js` →
   `kit`, `@sim/constants.js` → `constants`, `@sim/detmath.js` → `math` of `sp:sdk@1`), and the entry calls
   `sp.kits.register(<the record's new charId>, <kit>)` once per kit. A file whose imports do not map, that fails the
   registry's own `scriptCheck`, or that uses syntax the sandbox's module adapter refuses is named and not written.
   The plugin requires the content plugin at its exact version; a kit for an official id the pack declares in
   `overrides` registers that id and lists it in the manifest's `overrides`. It is checked with `checkPluginFolder`
   and `pack`, and never composed during the import (composition runs the preflight child). It runs only where the
   server owner set `SP_PLUGIN_SCRIPTS=sandbox` (or a trusted mode) and a room selects it — the report, the plugin's
   README, the studio dialog and PLUGINS.md §16 say so. `--no-scripts` and the studio's box 「不生成脚本插件」 leave it out.

The four examples after round 43 (dry run and a real import into a temporary `plugins/`, `check --strict` clean):
`my-first-pack` → the same cosmetic plugin; `clementia` → a 自选 record with `kit: { ref: "char_4231_clemnt" }`, her
three official skill ids, two talents and the trait (its art and voices are declared but the example ships no files);
`demo-workshop` → a tier-5 shop piece with `kit: { ref: "chess_char_5_01_a" }` (圣约送葬人's kit; the module,
statsBase / traitBase / talentsBase and `rangeId` are named); `kit-demo` → a tier-5 shop piece (the fallback bond, the
art of `char_1038_whitw2`) and the script plugin `kits-kit-demo`, which passes the script check and loads in the
sandbox. Its talent does not raise ATK here: the example adds its buff in `install` without `allowDead`, and the
engine — ours and Forge's, the same `addBuff` rule since the first import — refuses a buff on a unit that is not alive
yet (a kit is set up before the start). The report names such a kit; a synthetic kit with `persist` and `allowDead`
raises ATK by 25% in the same sandbox battle (`test/plugins/forgeImport.test.js`).

[ASSUMED] — round 43, for the owner to confirm or change: the script plugin's id `kits-<id>` (a `<id>-…` name would
share the content plugin's record namespace, which composition refuses); one script plugin per pack, one entry, the
Forge files carried rather than imported; the import table (`@kit/tier1.js` names of the SDK's 24 helpers,
`@sim/constants.js`, `@sim/detmath.js`; everything else refused, including `@kit/tier2–6`, `@kit/summoner.js`,
`@sim/dir.js`, `@sim/targeting.js` and `@ops/…`); a Forge tier1 helper is the SDK helper of that name, and the SDK's
frozen record copies are accepted; imports are initialization-time bindings; 64 files a kit and 1 MiB an entry; the
requirement on the content plugin's exact version; a kit registers the record's new charId (official overrides their
own id); a summon (`tokens`) kit is not converted; the `allowDead` text search is advice only; chess pairs: a `charId`
null takes official art named in `assets`; a pair with no bond at all gets 协防干员 (`economy.fallbackBondId`); one
